<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 524. UNIFORM PROBATE CODE</h2>
      
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.1-100"><b>524.1-100</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.001">15.001</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Article 1<br>GENERAL PROVISIONS,<br>DEFINITIONS AND PROBATE<br>JURISDICTION OF COURT<br>Part 1<br>CITATION, CONSTRUCTION,<br>GENERAL PROVISIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-101">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-101 CITATION AND NUMBERING SYSTEM.</h1>
        <p>This chapter shall be known and may be cited as the "Uniform Probate Code." It is arranged and numbered, subject however to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3C.10#stat.3C.10.1">3C.10, subdivision 1</a>, so that the enacted chapter may be compiled in the next published edition of Minnesota Statutes without change and in conformity with the official numbering of the Uniform Probate Code. The articles of Laws 1974, Chapter 442 are numbered out of sequence to facilitate the possible inclusion of other articles of the probate code in one chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-101; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/480/#laws.0.19.0">1984 c 480 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.2.19.0">1984 c 655 art 2 s 19</a> subd 7</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-102">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-102 PURPOSES; RULE OF CONSTRUCTION.</h1>
        <p>(a) This chapter and chapter 525 shall be liberally construed and applied to promote the underlying purposes and policies.</p>
        <p>(b) The underlying purposes and policies of this chapter and chapter 525 are:</p>
        <p>(1) to simplify and clarify the law concerning the affairs of decedents, missing persons, protected persons, minors and incapacitated persons;</p>
        <p>(2) to discover and make effective the intent of a decedent in distribution of property;</p>
        <p>(3) to promote a speedy and efficient system for liquidating the estate of the decedent and making distribution to successors;</p>
        <p>(4) to make uniform the law among the various jurisdictions.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-102.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-102; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.12.0">1975 c 347 s 12</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-103">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-103 SUPPLEMENTARY GENERAL PRINCIPLES OF LAW APPLICABLE.</h1>
        <p>Unless displaced by the particular provisions of this chapter, the principles of law and equity supplement its provisions.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-103.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-103</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-104">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-104 SEVERABILITY.</h1>
        <p>If any provision of this chapter or the application thereof to any person or circumstances is held invalid, the invalidity shall not affect other provisions or applications of the chapter which can be given effect without the invalid provision or application, and to this end the provisions of this chapter are declared to be severable.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-104.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-104</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.1-105"><b>524.1-105</b> MS 1974 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.144.0">1975 c 347 s 144</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-106">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-106 EFFECT OF FRAUD AND EVASION.</h1>
        <p>Whenever fraud has been perpetrated in connection with any proceeding or in any statement filed under this chapter or if fraud is used to avoid or circumvent the provisions or purposes of this chapter, any person injured thereby may obtain appropriate relief against the perpetrator of the fraud or restitution from any person, other than a bona fide purchaser, benefiting from the fraud, whether innocent or not. Any proceeding must be commenced within two years after the discovery of the fraud, but no proceeding may be brought against one not a perpetrator of the fraud later than five years after the time of commission of the fraud. This section has no bearing on remedies relating to fraud practiced on a decedent while living which affects the succession of the estate.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-106.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-106; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-107">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-107 EVIDENCE AS TO DEATH OR STATUS.</h1>
        <p>In proceedings under chapter 524 the Rules of Evidence in courts of general jurisdiction including any relating to simultaneous deaths, are applicable unless specifically displaced by this chapter. In addition, the following rules relating to determination of death and status are applicable:</p>
        <p>(1) a certified or authenticated copy of a death record purporting to be issued by an official or agency of the place where the death purportedly occurred is prima facie proof of the fact, place, date and time of death and the identity of the decedent;</p>
        <p>(2) a certified or authenticated copy of any record or report of a governmental agency, domestic or foreign, that a person is missing, detained, dead, or alive is prima facie evidence of the status and of the dates, circumstances and places disclosed by the record or report;</p>
        <p>(3) the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/578.16">578.16</a> shall govern the presumption of death of a person whose absence is not satisfactorily explained.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-107.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-107; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.13.0">1975 c 347 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.15.32.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 15 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.38.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-108">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-108 ACTS BY HOLDER OF GENERAL POWER.</h1>
        <p>For the purpose of granting consent or approval with regard to the acts or accounts of a personal representative or trustee, including relief from liability or penalty for failure to post bond or to perform other duties, and for purposes of consenting to modification or termination of a trust or to deviation from its terms, the sole holder or all coholders of a presently exercisable general power of appointment, including one in the form of a power of amendment or revocation, are deemed to act for beneficiaries to the extent their interests as objects, takers in default, or otherwise, are subject to the power.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-108.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-108; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.14.0">1975 c 347 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 2<br>DEFINITIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-201">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-201 GENERAL DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <p>Subject to additional definitions contained in the subsequent articles which are applicable to specific articles or parts, and unless the context otherwise requires, in chapters 524 and 525:</p>
        <p>(1) "Adoptee" means an individual who is adopted.</p>
        <p>(2) "Application" means a written request to the registrar for an order of informal probate or appointment under article III, part 3.</p>
        <p>(3) "Assisted reproduction" means a method of causing pregnancy other than sexual intercourse.</p>
        <p>(4) "Beneficiary," as it relates to trust beneficiaries, includes a person who has any present or future interest, vested or contingent, and also includes the owner of an interest by assignment or other transfer and as it relates to a charitable trust, includes any person entitled to enforce the trust.</p>
        <p>(5) "Birth mother" means a woman who gives birth to a child, including a woman who is the child's genetic mother and including a woman who gives birth to a child of assisted reproduction. "Birth mother" does not include a woman who gives birth pursuant to a gestational agreement.</p>
        <p>(6) "Child" includes any individual entitled to take as a child under law by intestate succession from the parent whose relationship is involved and excludes any person who is only a stepchild, a foster child, a grandchild or any more remote descendant.</p>
        <p>(7) "Child of assisted reproduction" means a child conceived by means of assisted reproduction by a woman other than a child conceived pursuant to a gestational agreement.</p>
        <p>(8) "Claims" includes liabilities of the decedent whether arising in contract or otherwise and liabilities of the estate which arise after the death of the decedent including funeral expenses and expenses of administration. The term does not include taxes, demands or disputes regarding title of a decedent to specific assets alleged to be included in the estate, tort claims, foreclosure of mechanic's liens, or to actions pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/573.02">573.02</a>.</p>
        <p>(9) "Court" means the court or branch having jurisdiction in matters relating to the affairs of decedents. This court in this state is known as the district court.</p>
        <p>(10) "Conservator" means a person who is appointed by a court to manage the estate of a protected person.</p>
        <p>(11) "Descendant" of an individual means all of the individual's descendants of all generations, with the relationship of parent and child at each generation being determined by the definition of child and parent contained in this section.</p>
        <p>(12) "Devise," when used as a noun, means a testamentary disposition of real or personal property and when used as a verb, means to dispose of real or personal property by will.</p>
        <p>(13) "Devisee" means any person designated in a will to receive a devise. In the case of a devise to an existing trust or trustee, or to a trustee on trust described by will, the trust or trustee is the devisee and the beneficiaries are not devisees.</p>
        <p>(14) "Disability" means cause for appointment of a conservator as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-401">524.5-401</a>, or a protective order as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-412">524.5-412</a>.</p>
        <p>(15) "Distributee" means any person who has received or who will receive property of a decedent from the decedent's personal representative other than as a creditor or purchaser. A testamentary trustee is a distributee with respect to property which the trustee has received from a personal representative only to the extent of distributed assets or their increment remaining in the trustee's hands. A beneficiary of a testamentary trust to whom the trustee has distributed property received from a personal representative is a distributee of the personal representative. For purposes of this provision, "testamentary trustee" includes a trustee to whom assets are transferred by will, to the extent of the devised assets.</p>
        <p>(16) "Divorce" includes an annulment, dissolution, and declaration of invalidity of marriage.</p>
        <p>(17) "Electronic will" means a will or codicil that (i) is created, signed, or maintained in an electronic, digital, magnetic, wireless, optical, electromagnetic, or other similar medium, (ii) is retrievable in perceivable form, and (iii) is capable of verification that the writing of the electronic will has not been altered after its signing.</p>
        <p>(18) "Estate" includes all of the property of the decedent, trust, or other person whose affairs are subject to this chapter as originally constituted and as it exists from time to time during administration.</p>
        <p>(19) "Fiduciary" includes personal representative, guardian, conservator and trustee.</p>
        <p>(20) "Foreign personal representative" means a personal representative of another jurisdiction.</p>
        <p>(21) "Formal proceedings" means those conducted before a judge with notice to interested persons.</p>
        <p>(22) "Functioned as a parent of the child" means behaving toward a child in a manner consistent with being the child's parent and performing functions that are customarily performed by a parent, including fulfilling parental responsibilities toward the child, recognizing or holding out the child as the individual's child, materially participating in the child's upbringing, and residing with the child in the same household as a regular member of that household.</p>
        <p>(23) "Genetic father" means the man whose sperm fertilized the egg of a child's genetic mother. If the father-child relationship is established under the presumption of paternity under chapter 257, "genetic father" means only the man for whom that relationship is established.</p>
        <p>(24) "Genetic mother" means the woman whose egg was fertilized by the sperm of a child's genetic father.</p>
        <p>(25) "Genetic parent" means a child's genetic father or genetic mother.</p>
        <p>(26) "Gestational agreement" means an agreement for assisted reproduction in which a woman agrees to carry a child to birth for an intended parent or intended parents.</p>
        <p>(27) "Governing instrument" means a deed; will; trust; insurance or annuity policy; account with POD designation; security registered in beneficiary form (TOD); transfer on death (TOD) deed; pension, profit-sharing, retirement, or similar benefit plan; instrument creating or exercising a power of appointment or a power of attorney; or a dispositive, appointive, or nominative instrument of any similar type.</p>
        <p>(28) "Guardian" means a person who has qualified as a guardian of a minor or incapacitated person pursuant to testamentary or court appointment, but excludes one who is merely a guardian ad litem.</p>
        <p>(29) "Heirs" means those persons, including the surviving spouse, who are entitled under the statutes of intestate succession to the property of a decedent.</p>
        <p>(30) "Incapacitated person" is as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-102#stat.524.5-102.6">524.5-102, subdivision 6</a>, other than a minor.</p>
        <p>(31) "Incapacity" when used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-114">524.2-114</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-120">524.2-120</a> means the inability of an individual to function as a parent of a child because of the individual's physical or mental condition.</p>
        <p>(32) "Informal proceedings" means those conducted by the judge, the registrar, or the person or persons designated by the judge for probate of a will or appointment of a personal representative in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-301">524.3-301</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-311">524.3-311</a>.</p>
        <p>(33) "Intended parent" means an individual who entered into a gestational agreement providing that the individual will be the parent of a child born to a woman by means of assisted reproduction, including an individual who has a genetic relationship with the child.</p>
        <p>(34) "Interested person" includes heirs, devisees, children, spouses, creditors, beneficiaries and any others having a property right in or claim against the estate of a decedent, ward or protected person which may be affected by the proceeding. It also includes persons having priority for appointment as personal representative, and other fiduciaries representing interested persons. The meaning as it relates to particular persons may vary from time to time and must be determined according to the particular purposes of, and matter involved in, any proceeding.</p>
        <p>(35) "Lease" includes an oil, gas, or other mineral lease.</p>
        <p>(36) "Letters" includes letters testamentary, letters of guardianship, letters of administration, and letters of conservatorship.</p>
        <p>(37) "Mortgage" means any conveyance, agreement or arrangement in which property is used as security.</p>
        <p>(38) "Nonresident decedent" means a decedent who was domiciled in another jurisdiction at the time of death.</p>
        <p>(39) "Organization" includes a corporation, government or governmental subdivision or agency, business trust, estate, trust, partnership or association, two or more persons having a joint or common interest, or any other legal entity.</p>
        <p>(40) "Person" means an individual, a corporation, an organization, or other legal entity.</p>
        <p>(41) "Personal representative" includes executor, administrator, successor personal representative, special administrator, and persons who perform substantially the same function under the law governing their status. "General personal representative" excludes special administrator.</p>
        <p>(42) "Petition" means a written request to the court for an order after notice.</p>
        <p>(43) "Presence" or "conscious presence" for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-501">524.2-501</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-517">524.2-517 </a>means either:</p>
        <p>(i) an individual being in a physical location in relation to the testator that allows the individual to see and hear the testator; or</p>
        <p>(ii) an individual being in a state and communicating simultaneously with the testator by means of an electronic device or process by sight and sound to substantially the same extent as if the individual were in a physical location in relation to the testator that would allow an individual to see and hear the testator, allowing for reasonable accommodation for individuals with hearing, vision, or speech impairments as necessary.</p>
        <p>(44) "Proceeding" includes action at law and suit in equity.</p>
        <p>(45) "Property" includes both real and personal property or any interest therein and means anything that may be the subject of ownership.</p>
        <p>(46) "Protected person" is as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-102#stat.524.5-102.14">524.5-102, subdivision 14</a>.</p>
        <p>(47) "Registrar" refers to the judge of the court or the person designated by the court to perform the functions of registrar as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.1-307">524.1-307</a>.</p>
        <p>(48) "Relative" means a grandparent or a descendant of a grandparent.</p>
        <p>(49) "Security" includes any note, stock, treasury stock, bond, debenture, evidence of indebtedness, certificate of interest or participation in an oil, gas or mining title or lease or in payments out of production under such a title or lease, collateral trust certificate, transferable share, voting trust certificate or, in general, any interest or instrument commonly known as a security, or any certificate of interest or participation, any temporary or interim certificate, receipt or certificate of deposit for, or any warrant or right to subscribe to or purchase, any of the foregoing.</p>
        <p>(50) "Settlement," in reference to a decedent's estate, includes the full process of administration, distribution and closing.</p>
        <p>(51) "Signed" or "signing" for testators and witnesses for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-501">524.2-501 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-517">524.2-517</a> means either:</p>
        <p>(i) the physical act of applying a signature or mark on the tangible medium upon which a writing is located; or</p>
        <p>(ii) to affix to or to logically associate with a writing an electronic symbol, signature, mark, or process with the intent to execute, witness, or authenticate the writing.</p>
        <p>(52) "Special administrator" means a personal representative as described by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-614">524.3-614</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-618">524.3-618</a>.</p>
        <p>(53) "State" includes any state of the United States, the District of Columbia, the Commonwealth of Puerto Rico, and any territory or possession subject to the legislative authority of the United States.</p>
        <p>(54) "Successor personal representative" means a personal representative, other than a special administrator, who is appointed to succeed a previously appointed personal representative.</p>
        <p>(55) "Successors" means those persons, other than creditors, who are entitled to property of a decedent under the decedent's will, this chapter or chapter 525. "Successors" also means a funeral director or county government that provides the funeral and burial of the decedent, or a state or county agency with a claim authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.15">256B.15</a>.</p>
        <p>(56) "Supervised administration" refers to the proceedings described in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-501">524.3-501</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-505">524.3-505</a>.</p>
        <p>(57) "Testacy proceeding" means a proceeding to establish a will or determine intestacy.</p>
        <p>(58) "Third-party donor" means an individual who produces eggs or sperm used for assisted reproduction, whether or not for consideration. The term does not include:</p>
        <p>(i) a spouse who provides sperm, or a spouse who provides eggs, that are used for assisted reproduction by the spouse;</p>
        <p>(ii) the birth mother of a child of assisted reproduction; or</p>
        <p>(iii) a man who has been determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-120#stat.524.2-120.4">524.2-120, subdivision 4</a> or 5, to have a parent-child relationship with a child of assisted reproduction.</p>
        <p>(59) "Trust" includes any express trust, private or charitable, with additions thereto, wherever and however created. It also includes a trust created or determined by judgment or decree under which the trust is to be administered in the manner of an express trust. "Trust" excludes other constructive trusts, and it excludes resulting trusts, conservatorships, personal representatives, trust accounts as defined in chapter 528, custodial arrangements pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.97">149A.97</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/318.01">318.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/318.06">318.06</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/527.21">527.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/527.44">527.44</a>, business trusts providing for certificates to be issued to beneficiaries, common trust funds, voting trusts, security arrangements, liquidation trusts, and trusts for the primary purpose of paying debts, dividends, interest, salaries, wages, profits, pensions, or employee benefits of any kind, and any arrangement under which a person is nominee or escrowee for another.</p>
        <p>(60) "Trustee" includes an original, additional, or successor trustee, whether or not appointed or confirmed by court.</p>
        <p>(61) "Ward" is as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-102#stat.524.5-102.17">524.5-102, subdivision 17</a>.</p>
        <p>(62) "Will" includes an electronic will, any codicil to a will or electronic will, and any testamentary instrument which merely appoints an executor or revokes or revises another will, electronic will, or codicil.</p>
        <p>(63) "Witnessing" for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-501">524.2-501</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-517">524.2-517</a> means observing or authenticating the testator's signing or acknowledgment of signing a will by individuals in the presence of the testator at the time of the testator's signing or acknowledgment of signing the will.</p>
        <p>(64) "Writing," "written instrument," or "written statement," for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.1-201">524.1-201</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-501">524.2-501</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-517">524.2-517</a>, means any reasonably permanent record that is readable as text at the time of signing and is retrievable in perceivable form, including any information that is: (i) inscribed on a tangible medium; or (ii) stored in an electronic, digital, magnetic, wireless, optical, electromagnetic, or other similar medium.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-201.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-201; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.15.0">1975 c 347 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.1.0">1978 c 525 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.1.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/423/#laws.0.2.0">1992 c 423 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 472 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/130/#laws.0.11.0">1995 c 130 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/186/#laws.0.119.0">1995 c 186 s 119</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/189/#laws.0.8.0">1995 c 189 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/277/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 277 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.45.0">1997 c 215 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/217/#laws.2.15.0">1997 c 217 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/146/#laws.3.40.0">2004 c 146 art 3 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.5.0">2010 c 334 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/135/#laws.2.21.0">2016 c 135 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/21/#laws.0.1.0">2023 c 21 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/101/#laws.3.2.0">2024 c 101 art 3 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 3<br>SCOPE, JURISDICTION<br>AND COURTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-301">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-301 TERRITORIAL APPLICATION.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise provided in this chapter, this chapter and chapter 525 apply to (1) the affairs and estates of decedents, missing persons, and persons to be protected, domiciled in this state, and (2) the property of nonresident decedents located in this state or property coming into the control of a fiduciary who is subject to the laws of this state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-301.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-301; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.16.0">1975 c 347 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-302">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-302 SUBJECT MATTER JURISDICTION.</h1>
        <p>(a) To the full extent permitted by the Constitution, the court has jurisdiction over all subject matter relating to estates of decedents, including construction of wills and determination of heirs and successors of decedents.</p>
        <p>(b) The court has full power to make orders, judgments and decrees and take all other action necessary and proper to administer justice in the matters which come before it.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-302.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-302; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.17.0">1975 c 347 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-303">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-303 VENUE; MULTIPLE PROCEEDINGS; TRANSFER.</h1>
        <p>(a) Where a proceeding under this chapter could be maintained in more than one place in this state, the court in which the proceeding is first commenced has the exclusive right to proceed.</p>
        <p>(b) If proceedings concerning the same estate, protected person, conservatee, or ward are commenced in more than one court of this state, the court in which the proceeding was first commenced shall continue to hear the matter, and the other courts shall hold the matter in abeyance until the question of venue is decided, and if the ruling court determines that venue is properly in another court, it shall transfer the proceeding to the other court.</p>
        <p>(c) If a court finds that in the interest of justice a proceeding or a file should be located in another court of this state, the court making the finding may transfer the proceeding or file to the other court.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-303.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-303; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.18.0">1975 c 347 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.1-304"><b>524.1-304</b> MS 1974 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.144.0">1975 c 347 s 144</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-304">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-304 PRACTICE IN COURT.</h1>
        <p>(a) Unless inconsistent with the provisions of this chapter or chapter 525, pleadings, practice, procedure and forms in all probate proceedings shall be governed insofar as practicable by Rules of Civil Procedure provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/484.81">484.81 </a>and adopted pursuant thereto.</p>
        <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), and in addition to its general powers, the court shall have power to correct, modify, vacate, or amend its records, orders, and decrees:</p>
        <p>(1) at any time, for the correction of clerical error or pursuant to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-413">524.3-413</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) within the time for taking an appeal, for the correction of judicial error;</p>
        <p>(3) within two years after petitioner's discovery thereof, for fraud, whether intrinsic or extrinsic, or misrepresentation unless petitioner be a party to such fraud; or</p>
        <p>(4) within two years after the date of filing of any record, order, or decree, for excusable neglect, inadvertence, or mistake.</p>
        <p class="bl">In any case, the petitioner must proceed with due diligence and may be barred by laches or the court may deny relief where it appears that the granting thereof would be inequitable in view of all the facts and circumstances appearing.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-304.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/157/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 157 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/117/#laws.1.1.0">2009 c 117 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.62.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 62</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.1-305"><b>524.1-305</b> MS 1974 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.144.0">1975 c 347 s 144</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-306">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-306 JURY TRIAL.</h1>
        <p>(a) If duly demanded, a party is entitled to trial by jury in any proceeding in which any controverted question of fact arises as to which any party has a constitutional right to trial by jury.</p>
        <p>(b) If there is no right to trial by jury under subsection (a) or the right is waived, the court in its discretion may call a jury to decide any issue of fact, in which case the verdict is advisory only.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-306.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-306</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-307">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-307 REGISTRAR; POWERS.</h1>
        <p>The acts and orders which this chapter specifies as performable by the registrar shall be performed by a judge of the court or by a person, including the court administrator, designated by the court by a written order filed and recorded in the office of the court.</p>
        <p>In addition to acts specified in this chapter to be performed by the registrar, the registrar may take acknowledgments, administer oaths, fix and approve bonds, provide information on the various methods of transferring property of decedents under the laws of this state, issue letters in informal proceedings and perform such other acts as the court may by written order authorize as necessary or incidental to the conduct of informal proceedings. Letters, orders and documents issued by the registrar may be certified, authenticated or exemplified by the registrar or in the same manner as those issued by the court. All files shall be maintained by the court administrator. The probate registrar shall not render advice calling for the exercise of such professional judgment as constitutes the practice of law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-307.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-307; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.19.0">1975 c 347 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/440/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 440 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-310">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-310 VERIFICATION OF FILED DOCUMENTS.</h1>
        <p>Every document filed with the court under this chapter or chapter 525 shall be verified except where the requirement of verification is waived by rule and except in the case of a pleading signed by an attorney in accordance with the Rules of Civil Procedure. Whenever a document is required to be verified:</p>
        <p>(1) such verification may be made by the unsworn written declaration of the party or parties signing the document that the representations made therein are known or believed to be true and that they are made under penalties for perjury, or</p>
        <p>(2) such verification may be made by the affidavit of the party or parties signing the document that the representations made therein are true or believed to be true.</p>
        <p>A party who makes a false material statement not believing it to be true in a document the party verifies in accordance with the preceding sentence and files with the court under this chapter or chapter 525 shall be subject to the penalties for perjury.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-310.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-310; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/161/#laws.0.3.0">1976 c 161 s 3</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 4<br>NOTICE, PARTIES AND<br>REPRESENTATION IN ESTATE<br>LITIGATION AND OTHER MATTERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-401">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-401 NOTICE; METHOD AND TIME OF GIVING.</h1>
        <p>(a) If notice of a hearing on any petition is required and except for specific notice requirements as otherwise provided, the petitioner shall cause notice of the time and place of hearing of any petition to be given to any interested person or the person's attorney if the person has appeared by attorney or requested that notice be sent to the attorney. Subject to paragraph (e), notice shall be given:</p>
        <p>(1) by mailing a copy thereof at least 14 days before the time set for the hearing by certified, registered or ordinary first class mail addressed to the person being notified at the post office address given in the demand for notice, if any, or at the demander's office or place of residence, if known;</p>
        <p>(2) by delivering a copy thereof to the person being notified personally at least 14 days before the time set for the hearing; or</p>
        <p>(3) if the address, or identity of any person is not known and cannot be ascertained with reasonable diligence, by publishing once a week for two consecutive weeks, a copy thereof in a legal newspaper in the county where the hearing is to be held, the last publication of which is to be at least 10 days before the time set for the hearing.</p>
        <p>(b) The court for good cause shown may provide for a different method or time of giving notice for any hearing.</p>
        <p>(c) Proof of the giving of notice shall be made on or before the hearing and filed in the proceeding.</p>
        <p>(d) No defect in any notice nor in publication or in service thereof shall limit or affect the validity of the appointment, powers, or other duties of the personal representative. Any of the notices required by this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-306">524.3-306</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-310">524.3-310</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-403">524.3-403</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-801">524.3-801</a> may be combined into one notice.</p>
        <p>(e) Except where personal service is required by statute for the petition to appoint a guardian under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-308">524.5-308</a> or conservator under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-404">524.5-404</a>, service of all documents and notices under this chapter may, and where required by Supreme Court rule or order shall, be made by electronic means other than facsimile transmission if authorized by rule or order of the Supreme Court and if service is made in accordance with the rule or order.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-401.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-401; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.20.0">1975 c 347 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/204/#laws.0.5.0">2014 c 204 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-402">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-402 NOTICE; WAIVER.</h1>
        <p>A person, including a guardian ad litem, conservator, or other fiduciary, may waive notice by a writing signed by the person or the person's attorney and filed in the proceeding.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-402.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-402; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-403">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-403 PLEADINGS; WHEN PARTIES BOUND BY OTHERS; NOTICE.</h1>
        <p>In formal proceedings involving estates of decedents and in judicially supervised settlements, the following apply:</p>
        <p>(1) Interests to be affected shall be described in pleadings which give reasonable information to owners by name or class, by reference to the instrument creating the interests, or in other appropriate manner.</p>
        <p>(2) Persons are bound by orders binding others in the following cases:</p>
        <p>(i) Orders binding the sole holder or all coholders of a power of revocation or a presently exercisable general power of appointment, including one in the form of a power of amendment, bind other persons to the extent their interests as objects, takers in default, or otherwise, are subject to the power.</p>
        <p>(ii) To the extent there is no conflict of interest between them or among persons represented, orders binding a conservator bind the person whose estate the conservator controls; orders binding a guardian bind the ward if no conservator of the estate has been appointed; orders binding a trustee bind beneficiaries of the trust in proceedings to probate a will establishing or adding to a trust, to review the acts or accounts of a prior fiduciary and in proceedings involving creditors or other third parties; and orders binding a personal representative bind persons interested in the undistributed assets of a decedent's estate in actions or proceedings by or against the estate. If there is no conflict of interest and no conservator or guardian has been appointed, a parent may represent the parent's minor child.</p>
        <p>(iii) An unborn or unascertained person who is not otherwise represented is bound by an order to the extent that person's interest is adequately represented by another party having a substantially identical interest in the proceeding.</p>
        <p>(3) Notice is required as follows:</p>
        <p>(i) Notice as prescribed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.1-401">524.1-401</a> shall be given to every interested person or to one who can bind an interested person as described in (2)(i) or (2)(ii). Notice may be given both to a person and to another who may bind the person.</p>
        <p>(ii) Notice is given to unborn or unascertained persons, who are not represented under (2)(i) or (2)(ii), by giving notice to all known persons whose interests in the proceedings are substantially identical to those of the unborn or unascertained persons.</p>
        <p>(4) At any point in a proceeding, a court may appoint a guardian ad litem to represent the interest of a minor, an incapacitated, unborn, or unascertained person, or a person whose identity or address is unknown, if the court determines that representation of the interest otherwise would be inadequate. If not precluded by conflict of interests, a guardian ad litem may be appointed to represent several persons or interests. The court shall set out its reasons for appointing a guardian ad litem as a part of the record of the proceeding.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-403.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/442/#laws.1.524.0">1974 c 442 art 1 s 524</a>.1-403; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.21.0">1975 c 347 s 21</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.1-404">
        <h1 class="shn">524.1-404 NOTICE TO CHARITABLE BENEFICIARIES.</h1>
        <p>If a will includes a gift, devise or bequest to a named charitable beneficiary, the initial written notice of the probate proceedings given to the beneficiary shall state that the beneficiary may request notice of the probate proceedings be given to the attorney general pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501B.41#stat.501B.41.5">501B.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.1-404.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/601/#laws.0.27.0">1978 c 601 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/340/#laws.2.3.0">1989 c 340 art 2 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Article 2<br>INTESTATE SUCCESSION AND WILLS<br>Part 1<br>INTESTATE SUCCESSION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-101">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-101 INTESTATE ESTATE.</h1>
        <p>(a) The intestate estate of the decedent consists of any part of the decedent's estate not allowed to the decedent's spouse or descendants under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-402">524.2-402</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-403">524.2-403</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-404">524.2-404</a>, and not disposed of by will. The intestate estate passes by intestate succession to the decedent's heirs as prescribed in this chapter, except as modified by the decedent's will.</p>
        <p>(b) A decedent by will may expressly exclude or limit the right of an individual or class to succeed to property of the decedent passing by intestate succession. If that individual or a member of that class survives the decedent, the share of the decedent's intestate estate to which that individual or class would have succeeded passes as if that individual or each member of that class had disclaimed an intestate share.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 250 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 472 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 171 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-102">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-102 SHARE OF THE SPOUSE.</h1>
        <p>The intestate share of a decedent's surviving spouse is:</p>
        <p>(1) the entire intestate estate if:</p>
        <p>(i) no descendant of the decedent survives the decedent; or</p>
        <p>(ii) all of the decedent's surviving descendants are also descendants of the surviving spouse and there is no other descendant of the surviving spouse who survives the decedent;</p>
        <p>(2) the first $225,000, plus one-half of any balance of the intestate estate, if all of the decedent's surviving descendants are also descendants of the surviving spouse and the surviving spouse has one or more surviving descendants who are not descendants of the decedent, or if one or more of the decedent's surviving descendants are not descendants of the surviving spouse.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-102.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.2.0">1985 c 250 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.3.0">1994 c 472 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/135/#laws.2.22.0">2016 c 135 art 2 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-103">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-103 SHARE OF HEIRS OTHER THAN SURVIVING SPOUSE.</h1>
        <p>Any part of the intestate estate not passing to the decedent's surviving spouse under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-102">524.2-102</a>, or the entire intestate estate if there is no surviving spouse, passes in the following order to the individuals designated below who survive the decedent:</p>
        <p>(1) to the decedent's descendants by representation;</p>
        <p>(2) if there is no surviving descendant, to the decedent's parents equally if both survive, or to the surviving parent;</p>
        <p>(3) if there is no surviving descendant or parent, to the descendants of the decedent's parents or either of them by representation;</p>
        <p>(4) if there is no surviving descendant, parent, or descendant of a parent, but the decedent is survived by one or more grandparents or descendants of grandparents, half of the estate passes to the decedent's paternal grandparents equally if both survive, or to the surviving paternal grandparent, or to the descendants of the decedent's paternal grandparents or either of them if both are deceased, the descendants taking by representation; and the other half passes to the decedent's maternal relatives in the same manner; but if there is no surviving grandparent or descendant of a grandparent on either the paternal or the maternal side, the entire estate passes to the decedent's relatives on the other side in the same manner as the half;</p>
        <p>(5) if there is no surviving descendant, parent, descendant of a parent, grandparent, or descendant of a grandparent, to the next of kin in equal degree, except that when there are two or more collateral kindred in equal degree claiming through different ancestors, those who claim through the nearest ancestor shall take to the exclusion of those claiming through an ancestor more remote.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-103.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.3.0">1985 c 250 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.4.0">1994 c 472 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-104">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-104 REQUIREMENT THAT HEIR SURVIVE DECEDENT FOR 120 HOURS.</h1>
        <p>An individual who fails to survive the decedent by 120 hours is deemed to have predeceased the decedent for purposes of homestead, exempt property, and intestate succession, and the decedent's heirs are determined accordingly. If it is not established that an individual who would otherwise be an heir survived the decedent by 120 hours, it is deemed that the individual failed to survive for the required period. This section is not to be applied if its application would result in a taking of intestate estate by the state under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-105">524.2-105</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-104.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.4.0">1985 c 250 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.5.0">1994 c 472 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-105">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-105 NO TAKER.</h1>
        <p>If there is no taker under the provisions of this article, the intestate estate passes to the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-105.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.5.0">1985 c 250 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.6.0">1994 c 472 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-106">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-106 REPRESENTATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) <b>Application.</b> If representation is called for by this article, paragraphs (b) and (c) apply.</p>
        <p>(b) <b>Decedent's descendants.</b> In the case of descendants of the decedent, the estate is divided into as many shares as there are surviving children of the decedent and deceased children who left descendants who survive the decedent, each surviving child receiving one share and the share of each deceased child being divided among its descendants in the same manner.</p>
        <p>(c) <b>Descendants of parents or grandparents.</b> If, under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-103">524.2-103</a>, clause (3) or (4), a decedent's intestate estate or a part thereof passes by "representation" to the descendants of the decedent's deceased parents or either of them or to the descendants of the decedent's deceased paternal or maternal grandparents or either of them, the estate or part thereof is divided in the following manner:</p>
        <p>(1) In the case of descendants of the decedent's deceased parents or either of them, the estate or part thereof is divided into as many equal shares as there are (i) surviving descendants in the generation nearest the deceased parents or either of them, and (ii) deceased descendants in the same generation who left surviving descendants, if any. Each surviving descendant in the nearest generation is allocated one share, and the surviving descendants of each deceased descendant in the same generation are allocated one share, to be divided in the same manner as specified in paragraph (b).</p>
        <p>(2) In the case of descendants of the decedent's deceased paternal or maternal grandparents or either of them, the estate or part thereof is divided into as many equal shares as there are surviving descendants in the generation nearest the deceased grandparents or either of them that contains one or more surviving descendants. Each surviving descendant in the nearest generation is allocated one share.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-106.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.6.0">1985 c 250 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.7.0">1994 c 472 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-107">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-107 DEGREE OF KINDRED AND KINDRED OF HALF BLOOD.</h1>
        <p>The degree of kindred shall be computed according to the rules of the civil law. Relatives of the half blood inherit the same share they would inherit if they were of the whole blood.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-107.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.7.0">1985 c 250 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-108">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-108 AFTER-BORN HEIRS.</h1>
        <p>An individual in gestation at a particular time is treated as living at that time if the individual lives 120 hours or more after birth.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-108.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.8.0">1985 c 250 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.8.0">1994 c 472 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-109">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-109 ADVANCEMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) If an individual dies intestate as to all or a portion of an estate, property the decedent gave during the decedent's lifetime to an individual who, at the decedent's death, is an heir is treated as an advancement against the heir's intestate share only if:</p>
        <p>(i) the decedent declared in a contemporaneous writing or the heir acknowledged in writing that the gift is an advancement; or</p>
        <p>(ii) the decedent's contemporaneous writing or the heir's written acknowledgment otherwise indicates that the gift is to be taken into account in computing the division and distribution of the decedent's intestate estate.</p>
        <p>(b) For purposes of paragraph (a), property advanced is valued as of the time the heir came into possession or enjoyment of the property or as of the time of the decedent's death, whichever first occurs.</p>
        <p>(c) If the recipient of the property fails to survive the decedent, the property is not taken into account in computing the division and distribution of the decedent's intestate estate, unless the decedent's contemporaneous writing provides otherwise.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-109.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.9.0">1985 c 250 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.9.0">1994 c 472 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-110">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-110 DEBTS TO DECEDENT.</h1>
        <p>A debt owed to a decedent is not charged against the intestate share of any individual except the debtor. If the debtor fails to survive the decedent, the debt is not taken into account in computing the intestate share of the debtor's descendants.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-110.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.10.0">1994 c 472 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-111">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-111 ALIENAGE.</h1>
        <p>No individual is disqualified to take as an heir because the individual or another through whom the individual claims is or has been an alien.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-111.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.10.0">1985 c 250 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.11.0">1994 c 472 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.2-112"><b>524.2-112</b> MS 1994 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.64.0">1994 c 472 s 64</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-113">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-113 INDIVIDUALS RELATED TO DECEDENT THROUGH TWO LINES.</h1>
        <p>An individual who is related to the decedent through two lines of relationship is entitled to only a single share based on the relationship that would entitle the individual to the larger share.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-113.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.12.0">1985 c 250 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.12.0">1994 c 472 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-114">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-114 PARENT BARRED FROM INHERITING IN CERTAIN CIRCUMSTANCES.</h1>
        <p>(a) A parent is barred from inheriting from or through a child of the parent if:</p>
        <p>(1) the parent's parental rights were terminated and the parent-child relationship was not judicially reestablished; or</p>
        <p>(2) the child died before reaching 18 years of age and there is clear and convincing evidence that immediately before the child's death the parental rights of the parent could have been terminated under law of this state other than this chapter on the basis of nonsupport, abandonment, abuse, neglect, or other actions or inactions of the parent toward the child.</p>
        <p>(b) For the purpose of intestate succession from or through the deceased child, a parent who is barred from inheriting under this section is treated as if the parent predeceased the child.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-114.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/250/#laws.0.13.0">1985 c 250 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.1.62.0">1994 c 465 art 1 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.13.0">1994 c 472 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/631/#laws.0.31.0">1994 c 631 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.1.75.0">2005 c 10 art 1 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/361/#laws.6.54.0">2008 c 361 art 6 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.6.0">2010 c 334 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.2-115"><b>524.2-115</b> [Renumbered 524.2-123]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-116">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-116 EFFECT OF PARENT-CHILD RELATIONSHIP.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-119">524.2-119, subdivisions 2</a> to 5, if a parent-child relationship exists or is established under this part, the parent is a parent of the child and the child is a child of the parent for the purpose of intestate succession.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-116.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.7.0">2010 c 334 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-117">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-117 PARENT-CHILD RELATIONSHIP WITH GENETIC PARENTS.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-114">524.2-114</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-119">524.2-119</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-120">524.2-120</a>, a parent-child relationship exists between a child and the child's genetic parents, regardless of the parents' marital status.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-117.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.8.0">2010 c 334 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-118">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-118 ADOPTEE AND ADOPTEE'S ADOPTIVE PARENT OR PARENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-118.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-118.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote"><b>Parent-child relationship between adoptee and adoptive parent or parents.</b></span></h2>
          <p>A parent-child relationship exists between an adoptee and the adoptee's adoptive parent or parents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-118.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-118.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote"><b>Individual in process of being adopted by married couple; stepchild in process of being adopted by stepparent.</b></span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of subdivision 1:</p>
          <p>(1) an individual who is in the process of being adopted by a married couple when one of the spouses dies is treated as adopted by the deceased spouse if the adoption is subsequently granted to the decedent's surviving spouse; and</p>
          <p>(2) a child of a genetic parent who is in the process of being adopted by a genetic parent's spouse when the spouse dies is treated as adopted by the deceased spouse if the genetic parent survives the deceased spouse by 120 hours.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-118.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-118.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote"><b>Child of assisted reproduction in process of being adopted.</b></span></h2>
          <p>If, after a parent-child relationship is established between a child of assisted reproduction and a parent under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-120">524.2-120</a>, the child is in the process of being adopted by the parent's spouse when that spouse dies, the child is treated as adopted by the deceased spouse for the purpose of subdivision 2, clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-118.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-118.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote"><b>In the process of adoption.</b></span></h2>
          <p>An individual is "in the process of being adopted" if there exists clear and convincing evidence of the intention of the deceased spouse to adopt that individual.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-118.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.9.0">2010 c 334 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-119">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-119 ADOPTEE AND ADOPTEE'S GENETIC PARENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-119.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-119.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote"><b>Parent-child relationship between adoptee and genetic parents.</b></span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in subdivisions 2 to 5, unless otherwise decreed, a parent-child relationship does not exist between an adoptee and the adoptee's genetic parents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-119.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-119.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote"><b>Stepchild adopted by stepparent.</b></span></h2>
          <p>A parent-child relationship exists between an individual who is adopted by the spouse of either genetic parent and the genetic parent whose spouse adopted the individual. No parent-child relationship exists between an individual and the other genetic parent unless the other genetic parent was deceased at the time of the child's adoption and then only for the purpose of the right of the adoptee or a descendant of the adoptee to inherit from or through that other genetic parent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-119.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-119.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote"><b>Individual adopted by relative of genetic parent.</b></span></h2>
          <p>A parent-child relationship exists between both genetic parents and an individual who is adopted by a relative of a genetic parent, or by the spouse or surviving spouse of a relative of a genetic parent, but only for the purpose of the right of the adoptee or a descendant of the adoptee to inherit from or through either genetic parent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-119.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-119.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote"><b>Individual adopted after death of both genetic parents.</b></span></h2>
          <p>A parent-child relationship exists between both genetic parents and an individual who is adopted after the death of both genetic parents, but only for the purpose of the right of the adoptee or a descendant of the adoptee to inherit through either genetic parent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-119.5">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-119.5">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote"><b>Child of assisted reproduction who is subsequently adopted.</b></span></h2>
          <p>If, after a parent-child relationship is established between a child of assisted reproduction and a parent or parents under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-120">524.2-120</a>, the child is adopted by another or others, the child's parent or parents under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-120">524.2-120</a> are treated as the child's genetic parent or parents for the purpose of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-119.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.10.0">2010 c 334 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-120">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-120 CHILD CONCEIVED BY ASSISTED REPRODUCTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote"><b>Third-party donor.</b></span></h2>
          <p>A parent-child relationship does not exist between a child of assisted reproduction and a third-party donor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote"><b>Parent-child relationship with birth mother.</b></span></h2>
          <p>A parent-child relationship exists between a child of assisted reproduction and the child's birth mother.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote"><b>Parent-child relationship with husband whose sperm were used during his lifetime by his wife for assisted reproduction.</b></span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in subdivision 9, a parent-child relationship exists between a child of assisted reproduction and the husband of the child's birth mother if the husband provided the sperm that the birth mother used during his lifetime for assisted reproduction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote"><b>Official birth record; presumptive effect.</b></span></h2>
          <p>An official birth record identifying a man as the other parent of a child of assisted reproduction presumptively establishes a parent-child relationship between the child and that man.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.5">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.5">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote"><b>Parent-child relationship with another.</b></span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in subdivisions 6, 8, and 9, and unless a parent-child relationship is established under subdivision 4, a parent-child relationship is presumed to exist between a child of assisted reproduction and a man who consented to assisted reproduction by the birth mother with intent to be treated as the other parent of the child. Consent to assisted reproduction by the birth mother with intent to be treated as the other parent of the child is established if the man:</p>
          <p>(1) before or after the child's birth, signed a record that, considering all the facts and circumstances, evidences the man's consent; or</p>
          <p>(2) in the absence of a signed record under clause (1):</p>
          <p>(i) functioned as the other parent of the child no later than two years after the child's birth; or</p>
          <p>(ii) intended to function as the other parent of the child no later than two years after the child's birth but was prevented from carrying out that intent by death, incapacity, or other circumstances, if that intent is established by clear and convincing evidence.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.6">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 6." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.6">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote"><b>Effect of record signed more than two years after the birth of the child.</b></span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of subdivision 5, clause (1), neither a man who signed a record more than two years after the birth of the child, nor a relative of that man who is not also a relative of the birth mother, inherits from or through the child unless the man functioned as a parent of the child before the child reached 18 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.7">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 7." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.7">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote"><b>Presumption; birth mother is married or surviving spouse.</b></span></h2>
          <p>(a) Paragraphs (b) and (c) apply to subdivision 5, clause (2).</p>
          <p>(b) If the birth mother is married and no divorce proceeding is pending, in the absence of clear and convincing evidence to the contrary, her spouse satisfies subdivision 5, clause (2), item (i) or (ii).</p>
          <p>(c) If the birth mother is a surviving spouse and at her deceased spouse's death no divorce proceeding was pending, in the absence of clear and convincing evidence to the contrary, her deceased spouse satisfies subdivision 5, clause (2), item (ii).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.8">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 8." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.8">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote"><b>Divorce before placement of eggs, sperm, or embryos.</b></span></h2>
          <p>If a married couple is divorced before placement of eggs, sperm, or embryos, a child resulting from the assisted reproduction is not a child of the birth mother's former spouse, unless the former spouse consented in a record or such consent is established by clear and convincing evidence that if assisted reproduction were to occur after divorce, the child would be treated as the former spouse's child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.9">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 9." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.9">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote"><b>Withdrawal of consent before placement of eggs, sperm, or embryos.</b></span></h2>
          <p>If, in a record or through clear and convincing evidence, a man withdraws consent to assisted reproduction before placement of eggs, sperm, or embryos, a child resulting from the assisted reproduction is not a child of that man, unless the man subsequently satisfies subdivision 4 or 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-120.10">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 10." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-120.10">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote"><b>Exclusion of posthumously conceived children.</b></span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other provision of this section and subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-108">524.2-108</a>, a parent-child relationship does not exist between a child of assisted reproduction and another person unless the child of assisted reproduction is in gestation prior to the death of such person.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-120.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.11.0">2010 c 334 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-121">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-121 NO EFFECT ON GESTATIONAL AGREEMENTS.</h1>
        <p>This chapter does not affect law of this state regarding gestational agreements.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-121.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.12.0">2010 c 334 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-122">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-122 NO EFFECT ON EQUITABLE ADOPTION.</h1>
        <p>This chapter does not affect the doctrine of equitable adoption.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-122.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.13.0">2010 c 334 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-123">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-123 INSTRUMENTS REFERENCING INTESTACY LAWS.</h1>
        <p>If a maker has executed a will or other instrument before January 1, 1996, which directs disposition of all or part of the estate pursuant to the intestacy laws of the state of Minnesota, the laws to be applied shall be in accordance with the laws of intestate succession in effect on the date of the will or other instrument, unless the will or instrument directs otherwise.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-123.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.14.0">1994 c 472 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.16.0">2010 c 334 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 2<br>ELECTIVE SHARE OF<br>SURVIVING SPOUSE</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.2-201"><b>524.2-201</b> MS 1992 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.64.0">1994 c 472 s 64</a>]</div>
