        <p>(c) If a subsequent will that revoked a previous will in whole or in part is thereafter revoked by another later will, the previous will remains revoked in whole or in part, unless it or its revoked part is revived. The previous will or its revoked part is revived to the extent it appears from the terms of the later will that the testator intended the previous will to take effect.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-509.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.41.0">1994 c 472 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-510">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-510 INCORPORATION BY REFERENCE.</h1>
        <p>Any writing in existence when a will is executed may be incorporated by reference if the language of the will manifests this intent and describes the writing sufficiently to permit its identification.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-510.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-511">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-511 TESTAMENTARY ADDITIONS TO TRUSTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) A will may validly devise property to the trustee of a trust established or to be established (i) during the testator's lifetime by the testator, by the testator and some other person, or by some other person, including a funded or unfunded life insurance trust, although the settlor has reserved any or all rights of ownership of the insurance contracts, or (ii) at the testator's death by the testator's devise to the trustee, if, in either case, the trust is identified in the testator's will and its terms are set forth in a written instrument, other than a will, executed before, concurrently with, or after the execution of the testator's will or in another individual's will if that other individual has predeceased the testator, regardless of the existence, size, or character of the corpus of the trust. The devise is not invalid because the trust is amendable or revocable, or because the trust was amended after the execution of the will or the testator's death.</p>
        <p>(b) Unless the testator's will provides otherwise, property devised to a trust described in paragraph (a) is not held under a testamentary trust of the testator, but it becomes a part of the trust to which it is devised, and must be administered and disposed of in accordance with the provisions of the governing instrument setting forth the terms of the trust, including any amendments thereto made before or after the testator's death.</p>
        <p>(c) Unless the testator's will provides otherwise, a revocation or termination of the trust before the testator's death causes the devise to lapse.</p>
        <p>(d) This section does not invalidate a devise made by a will executed before February 21, 1963.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-511.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.42.0">1994 c 472 s 42</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-512">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-512 EVENTS OF INDEPENDENT SIGNIFICANCE.</h1>
        <p>A will may dispose of property by reference to acts and events that have significance apart from their effect upon the dispositions made by the will, whether they occur before or after the execution of the will or before or after the testator's death. The execution or revocation of another individual's will is such an event.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-512.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.43.0">1994 c 472 s 43</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-513">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-513 SEPARATE WRITING IDENTIFYING BEQUEST OF TANGIBLE PROPERTY.</h1>
        <p>A will may refer to a written statement or list to dispose of items of tangible personal property not otherwise specifically disposed of by the will, other than money and coin collections, and property used in trade or business. To be admissible under this section as evidence of the intended disposition, the writing must be referred to in the will, must be either in the handwriting of the testator or be signed by the testator, and must describe the items and the devisees with reasonable certainty. The writing may be referred to as one to be in existence at the time of the testator's death; it may be prepared before or after the execution of the will; it may be altered by the testator after its preparation; and it may be a writing which has no significance apart from its effect upon the dispositions made by the will.</p>
        <p>A writing may include multiple writings and if an item of tangible personal property is disposed of to different persons by different writings, the most recent writing controls the disposition of the item.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-513.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/362/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 362 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-514">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-514 CONTRACTS CONCERNING SUCCESSION.</h1>
        <p>A contract to make a will or devise, or not to revoke a will or devise, or to die intestate, if executed after January 1, 1976, may be established only by (i) provisions of a will stating material provisions of the contract, (ii) an express reference in a will to a contract and extrinsic evidence proving the terms of the contract, or (iii) a writing signed by the decedent evidencing the contract. The execution of a joint will or mutual wills does not create a presumption of a contract not to revoke the will or wills.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-514.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.44.0">1994 c 472 s 44</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-515">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-515 DEPOSIT OF WILL WITH COURT IN TESTATOR'S LIFETIME.</h1>
        <p>A will may be deposited by the testator or the testator's agent with any court for safekeeping, under rules of the court. The will must be sealed and kept confidential. During the testator's lifetime, a deposited will must be delivered only to the testator or to a person authorized in writing signed by the testator to receive the will. A conservator or guardian may be allowed to examine a deposited will of a protected testator under procedures designed to maintain the confidential character of the document to the extent possible, and to ensure that it will be resealed and kept on deposit after the examination. Upon being informed of the testator's death, the court may deliver the will to the appropriate court.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-515.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.45.0">1994 c 472 s 45</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-516">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-516 DUTY OF CUSTODIAN OF WILL; LIABILITY.</h1>
        <p>After the death of a testator and on request of an interested person, a person having custody of a will of the testator shall deliver it with reasonable promptness to an appropriate court. A person who willfully fails to deliver a will is liable to any person aggrieved for any damages that may be sustained by the failure. A person who willfully refuses or fails to deliver a will after being ordered by the court in a proceeding brought for the purpose of compelling delivery is subject to penalty for contempt of court.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-516.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.46.0">1994 c 472 s 46</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-517">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-517 PENALTY CLAUSE FOR CONTEST.</h1>
        <p>A provision in a will purporting to penalize an interested person for contesting the will or instituting other proceedings relating to the estate is unenforceable if probable cause exists for instituting proceedings.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-517.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.47.0">1994 c 472 s 47</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-518">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-518 CERTIFICATION OF PAPER COPY.</h1>
        <p>An individual may create a certified paper copy of an electronic will by affirming under penalty of perjury that a paper copy of the electronic will is a complete, true, and accurate copy of the electronic will. If the electronic will is made self-proving, the certified paper copy of the will must include the self-proving affidavits. A certified paper copy of an electronic will may be substituted for an original will when an original will is required by this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-518.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/21/#laws.0.5.0">2023 c 21 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 6<br>RULES OF CONSTRUCTION APPLICABLE ONLY TO WILLS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-601">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-601 SCOPE.</h1>
        <p>In the absence of a finding of a contrary intention, the rules of construction in this part control the construction of a will.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-601.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.48.0">1994 c 472 s 48</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-602">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-602 WILL MAY PASS ALL PROPERTY AND AFTER-ACQUIRED PROPERTY.</h1>
        <p>A will may provide for the passage of all property the testator owns at death and all property acquired by the estate after the testator's death.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-602.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.49.0">1994 c 472 s 49</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.2-603"><b>524.2-603</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/15/#laws.0.14.0">2001 c 15 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-603">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-603 ANTILAPSE; DECEASED DEVISEE; CLASS GIFTS; WORDS OF SURVIVORSHIP.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-603.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-603.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Deceased devisee.</span></h2>
          <p>If a devisee who is a grandparent or a lineal descendant of a grandparent of the testator is dead at the time of execution of the will, fails to survive the testator, or is treated as if the devisee predeceased the testator, the issue of the deceased devisee who survive the testator by 120 hours take in place of the deceased devisee. If they are all of the same degree of kinship to the devisee, they take equally. If they are of unequal degree, those of more remote degree take by representation. A person who would have been a devisee under a class gift if the person had survived the testator is treated as a devisee for purposes of this section, whether the death occurred before or after the execution of the will.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-603.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-603.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-601">524.2-601</a>, words of survivorship, such as, in a devise to an individual, "if he or she survives me," or, in a class gift, to "my surviving children," are a sufficient indication of an intent contrary to the application of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-603.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/15/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 15 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-604">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-604 FAILURE OF TESTAMENTARY PROVISION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-603">524.2-603</a>, a devise, other than a residuary devise, that fails for any reason becomes a part of the residue.</p>
        <p>(b) Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-603">524.2-603</a>, if the residue is devised to two or more persons, the share of a residuary devisee that fails for any reason passes to the other residuary devisee, or to other residuary devisees in proportion to the interest of each in the remaining part of the residue.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-604.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.51.0">1994 c 472 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.102.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 102</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-605">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-605 INCREASE IN SECURITIES; ACCESSIONS.</h1>
        <p>(a) If a testator executes a will that devises securities and the testator then owned securities that meet the description in the will, the devise includes additional securities owned by the testator at death to the extent the additional securities were acquired by the testator after the will was executed as a result of the testator's ownership of the described securities and are securities of any of the following types:</p>
        <p>(1) securities of the same organization acquired by reason of action initiated by the organization or any successor, related, or acquiring organization, excluding any acquired by exercise of purchase options;</p>
        <p>(2) securities of another organization acquired as a result of a merger, consolidation, reorganization, or other distribution by the organization or any successor, related, or acquiring organization; or</p>
        <p>(3) securities of the same organization acquired as a result of a plan of reinvestment.</p>
        <p>(b) Distributions in cash before death with respect to a described security are not part of the devise.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-605.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.52.0">1994 c 472 s 52</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-606">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-606 NONADEMPTION OF SPECIFIC DEVISES; UNPAID PROCEEDS OF SALE, CONDEMNATION, OR INSURANCE; SALE BY CONSERVATOR OR GUARDIAN.</h1>
        <p>(a) A specific devisee has a right to the specifically devised property in the testator's estate at death and:</p>
        <p>(1) any balance of the purchase price, together with any security agreement, owing from a purchaser to the testator at death by reason of sale of the property;</p>
        <p>(2) any amount of a condemnation award for the taking of the property unpaid at death;</p>
        <p>(3) any proceeds unpaid at death on fire or casualty insurance on or other recovery for injury to the property; and</p>
        <p>(4) property owned by the testator at death and acquired as a result of foreclosure, or obtained in lieu of foreclosure, of the security interest for a specifically devised obligation.</p>
        <p>(b) If specifically devised property is sold or mortgaged by a conservator or guardian, by an agent acting within the authority of a durable power of attorney for an incapacitated principal, or by the trustee of a revocable trust during the period of the settlor's incapacity, or if a condemnation award, insurance proceeds, or recovery for injury to the property are paid to a conservator or guardian, to an agent acting within the authority of a durable power of attorney for an incapacitated principal, or to the trustee of a revocable trust during the period of the settlor's incapacity, the specific devisee has the right to a general pecuniary devise equal to the net sale price, the amount of the unpaid loan, the condemnation award, the insurance proceeds, or the recovery.</p>
        <p>(c) The right of a specific devisee under paragraph (b) is reduced by any right the devisee has under paragraph (a).</p>
        <p>(d) For the purposes of the references in paragraph (b) to a conservator or guardian or an agent acting within the authority of a durable power of attorney or a trustee of a revocable trust during the period of the settlor's incapacity, paragraph (b) does not apply if after the sale, mortgage, condemnation, casualty, or recovery:</p>
        <p>(1) in the case of a conservator or guardian, it was adjudicated that the testator's incapacity ceased and the testator survived the adjudication by one year;</p>
        <p>(2) in the case of an agent acting within the authority of a durable power of attorney, the testator's incapacity ceased and the testator survived for one year after the incapacity ceased; or</p>
        <p>(3) in the case of a trustee, the settlor's incapacity ceased and the settlor survived for one year after the incapacity ceased.</p>
        <p>(e) For the purposes of the references in paragraph (b) to the trustee of a revocable trust during the period of the settlor's incapacity, paragraph (b) does not apply to a specific devise contained in a will if:</p>
        <p>(1) the revocable trust provides for the transfer, devise, or distribution of all trust assets held as of the death of the settlor to persons or entities other than the settlor's estate; and</p>
        <p>(2) the initial transfer of devised property into the trust occurred prior to the settlor's incapacity.</p>
        <p>(f) For the purposes of the references in paragraph (b) to an agent acting within the authority of a durable power of attorney for an incapacitated principal or the trustee of a revocable trust during the period of the settlor's incapacity, (i) "incapacitated principal" means a principal who is an incapacitated person as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-102#stat.524.5-102.6">524.5-102, subdivision 6</a>, and the "period of the settlor's incapacity" means a period when the settlor of a revocable trust is an incapacitated person as defined by the trust instrument, or, if the trust instrument is silent, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-102#stat.524.5-102.6">524.5-102, subdivision 6</a>, and (ii) a finding of the principal's or settlor's incapacity need not occur during the principal's or settlor's life.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-606.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.53.0">1994 c 472 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/9/#laws.0.8.0">1997 c 9 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/146/#laws.3.41.0">2004 c 146 art 3 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/135/#laws.2.27.0">2016 c 135 art 2 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-607">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-607 NONEXONERATION.</h1>
        <p>A specific devise passes subject to any mortgage or security interest existing at the date of death, without right of exoneration, regardless of a general directive in the will to pay debts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-607.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.54.0">1994 c 472 s 54</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-608">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-608 EXERCISE OF POWER OF APPOINTMENT.</h1>
        <p>A general residuary clause in a will, or a will making general disposition of all of the testator's property, does not exercise a power of appointment held by the testator unless the testator's will manifests an intention to include property subject to the power.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-608.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.55.0">1994 c 472 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-609">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-609 ADEMPTION BY SATISFACTION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Property a testator, while living, gave to a person is treated as a satisfaction of a devise in whole or in part, only if (i) the will provides for deduction of the gift, (ii) the testator declared in a contemporaneous writing that the gift is in satisfaction of the devise or that its value is to be deducted from the value of the devise, or (iii) the devisee acknowledged in writing that the gift is in satisfaction of the devise or that its value is to be deducted from the value of the devise.</p>
        <p>(b) For purposes of partial satisfaction, property given during lifetime is valued as of the time the devisee came into possession or enjoyment of the property or at the testator's death, whichever occurs first.</p>
        <p>(c) If the devisee fails to survive the testator, the gift is treated as a full or partial satisfaction of the devise, as appropriate, in applying sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-603">524.2-603</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-604">524.2-604</a>, unless the testator's contemporaneous writing provides otherwise.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-609.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.56.0">1994 c 472 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.103.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 103</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.2-610"><b>524.2-610</b> MS 1994 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.64.0">1994 c 472 s 64</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.524.2-612"><b>524.2-612</b> MS 1994 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.64.0">1994 c 472 s 64</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 7<br>RULES OF CONSTRUCTION APPLICABLE TO WILLS<br>AND OTHER GOVERNING INSTRUMENTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-701">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-701 SCOPE.</h1>
        <p>In the absence of a finding of a contrary intention, the rules of construction in this part control the construction of a governing instrument. The rules of construction in this part apply to a governing instrument of any type, except as the application of a particular section is limited by its terms to a specific type or types of provision or governing instrument.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-701.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.57.0">1994 c 472 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-702">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-702 REQUIREMENT OF SURVIVAL FOR 120 HOURS FOR DEVISEES, BENEFICIARIES OF CERTAIN TRUSTS, AND APPOINTEES OF CERTAIN POWERS OF APPOINTMENT; SIMULTANEOUS DEATH ACT FOR OTHER CASES.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) <b>Requirement of survival for 120 hours.</b> A beneficiary of a trust in which the grantor has reserved a power to alter, amend, revoke, or terminate the provisions of the trust who fails to survive the grantor by 120 hours, a devisee who fails to survive the testator by 120 hours, a beneficiary named in a transfer on death deed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/507.071">507.071</a> who fails to survive by 120 hours the grantor owner upon whose death the conveyance to the beneficiary becomes effective, or an appointee of a power of appointment taking effect at the death of the holder of the power who fails to survive the holder of the power by 120 hours is deemed to have predeceased the grantor, grantor owner testator, or holder of the power for purposes of determining title to property passing by the trust instrument, by the testator's will, by the transfer on death deed, or by the exercise of the power of appointment.</p>
        <p class="in">(b)(1) <b>Title to property in other cases.</b> In cases not governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-104">524.2-104 </a>or paragraph (a), where the title to property or the devolution thereof depends upon priority of death and there is no sufficient evidence that the persons have died otherwise than simultaneously, the property of each person shall be disposed of as if the person had survived, except as provided otherwise in this paragraph.</p>
        <p class="in">(2) <b>Death of multiple beneficiaries; division of property.</b> Where two or more beneficiaries are designated to take successively by reason of survivorship under another person's disposition of property and there is no sufficient evidence that these beneficiaries have died otherwise than simultaneously the property thus disposed of shall be divided into as many equal portions as there are successive beneficiaries and these portions shall be distributed respectively to those who would have taken in the event that each designated beneficiary had survived.</p>
        <p class="in">(3) <b>Death of joint tenants or tenants by the entirety; division of property.</b> Where there is no sufficient evidence that two joint tenants or tenants by the entirety have died otherwise than simultaneously the property so held shall be distributed one-half as if one had survived and one-half as if the other had survived. If there are more than two joint tenants and all of them have so died the property thus distributed shall be in the proportion that one bears to the whole number of joint tenants.</p>
        <p class="in">(4) <b>Death of insured and beneficiary; division of property.</b> Where the insured and the beneficiary in a policy of life or accident insurance have died and there is no sufficient evidence that they have died otherwise than simultaneously the proceeds of the policy shall be distributed as if the insured had survived the beneficiary.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) <b>Not retroactive.</b> This section does not apply to the distribution of the property of a person who has died before it takes effect. Paragraph (a) applies only to persons who die on or after August 1, 1999.</p>
        <p class="in">(d) <b>Application.</b> This section does not apply in the case of wills, trusts, deeds, contracts of insurance, or documents exercising powers of appointment wherein provision has been made for distribution of property different from the provisions of this section. Paragraph (a) does not apply to trusts which are part of a qualified or nonqualified retirement plan or individual retirement accounts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-702.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/248/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 248 s 1</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/171/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 171 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/341/#laws.2.8.0">2008 c 341 art 2 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-703">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-703 CHOICE OF LAW AS TO MEANING AND EFFECT OF GOVERNING INSTRUMENT.</h1>
        <p>The meaning and legal effect of a governing instrument is determined by the local law of the state selected in the governing instrument, unless the application of that law is contrary to the provisions relating to the elective share described in part 2, the provisions relating to exempt property and allowances described in part 4, or any other public policy of this state otherwise applicable to the disposition.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-703.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.58.0">1994 c 472 s 58</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-704">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-704 POWER OF APPOINTMENT; MEANING OF SPECIFIC REFERENCE REQUIREMENT.</h1>
        <p>If a governing instrument creating a power of appointment expressly requires that the power be exercised by a reference, an express reference, or a specific reference, to the power or its source, it is presumed that the donor's intention, in requiring that the donee exercise the power by making reference to the particular power or to the creating instrument, was to prevent an inadvertent exercise of the power and an attempt to exercise the power by a donee who had knowledge of and intended to exercise the power is effective.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-704.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.59.0">1994 c 472 s 59</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-705">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-705 CLASS GIFTS CONSTRUED TO ACCORD WITH INTESTATE SUCCESSION.</h1>
        <p>Adopted individuals and individuals born out of wedlock, and their respective descendants if appropriate to the class, are included in class gifts and other terms of relationship in accordance with the rules for intestate succession. Terms of relationship that do not differentiate relationships by blood from those by affinity, such as "uncles," "aunts," "nieces," or "nephews," are presumed to exclude relatives by affinity. Terms of relationship that do not differentiate relationships by the half blood from those by the whole blood, such as "brothers," "sisters," "nieces," or "nephews," are presumed to include both types of relationships.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-705.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.60.0">1994 c 472 s 60</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-708">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-708 CLASS GIFTS TO "DESCENDANTS," "ISSUE," OR "HEIRS OF THE BODY"; FORM OF DISTRIBUTION IF NONE SPECIFIED.</h1>
        <p>If a class gift in favor of "descendants," "issue," or "heirs of the body" does not specify the manner in which the property is to be distributed among the class members, the property is distributed among the class members who are living when the interest is to take effect in possession or enjoyment, in such shares as they would receive, under the applicable law of intestate succession, if the designated ancestor had then died intestate owning the subject matter of the class gift.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-708.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.61.0">1994 c 472 s 61</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-709">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-709 REPRESENTATION; PER STIRPES; PER CAPITA AT EACH GENERATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) <b>Definitions.</b> In this section:</p>
        <p>(1) "Deceased child" or "deceased descendant" means a child or a descendant who either predeceased the distribution date or is deemed to have predeceased the distribution date under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-702">524.2-702</a>.</p>
        <p>(2) "Distribution date," with respect to an interest, means the time when the interest is to take effect in possession or enjoyment. The distribution date need not occur at the beginning or end of a calendar day, but can occur at a time during the course of a day.</p>
        <p>(3) "Surviving ancestor," "surviving child," or "surviving descendant" means an ancestor, a child, or a descendant who neither predeceased the distribution date nor is deemed to have predeceased the distribution date under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-702">524.2-702</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) <b>Representation; per stirpes.</b> If an applicable statute or governing instrument calls for property to be distributed by "representation" or "per stirpes," the property is divided into as many equal shares as there are (i) surviving children of the designated ancestor and (ii) deceased children who left surviving descendants. Each surviving child, if any, is allocated one share. The share of each deceased child with surviving descendants is divided in the same manner, with subdivision repeating at each succeeding generation until the property is fully allocated among surviving descendants.</p>
        <p>(c) <b>Per capita at each generation.</b> If a governing instrument calls for property to be distributed "per capita at each generation," the property is divided into as many equal shares as there are (i) surviving descendants in the generation nearest to the designated ancestor which contains one or more surviving descendants and (ii) deceased descendants in the same generation who left surviving descendants, if any. Each surviving descendant in the nearest generation is allocated one share. The remaining shares, if any, are combined and then divided in the same manner among the surviving descendants of the deceased descendants as if the surviving descendants who were allocated a share and their surviving descendants had predeceased the distribution date.</p>
        <p>(d) <b>Deceased descendant with no surviving descendant disregarded.</b> For the purposes of paragraphs (b) and (c), an individual who is deceased and left no surviving descendant is disregarded, and an individual who leaves a surviving ancestor who is a descendant of the designated ancestor is not entitled to a share.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-709.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.62.0">1994 c 472 s 62</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-711">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-711 FUTURE INTERESTS IN "HEIRS," "HEIRS AT LAW," OR "NEXT OF KIN."</h1>
        <p>If a governing instrument calls for a future distribution to or creates a future interest in a designated individual's "heirs," "heirs at law," or "next of kin," the property passes to those persons, including the state of Minnesota under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-105">524.2-105</a>, and in such shares as would succeed to the designated individual's intestate estate under the laws of intestate succession of the state of Minnesota if the designated individual died when the disposition is to take effect in possession or enjoyment. If the designated individual's surviving spouse is living at the time the disposition is to take effect in possession or enjoyment, the surviving spouse is an heir of the designated individual for the purposes of this section, whether or not the surviving spouse is remarried.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-711.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/9/#laws.0.9.0">1997 c 9 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-712">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-712 DECEDENTS DYING AFTER DECEMBER 31, 2009, AND BEFORE JANUARY 1, 2011; FORMULA CLAUSES TO BE CONSTRUED TO REFER TO FEDERAL ESTATE TAX AND FEDERAL GENERATION-SKIPPING TRANSFER TAX LAWS.</h1>
        <p>(a) A governing instrument, including a will or trust agreement, of a decedent who dies after December 31, 2009, and before January 1, 2011, that contains a formula or provision referring to the "unified credit," "estate tax exemption," "applicable exemption amount," "applicable credit amount," "applicable exclusion amount," "generation-skipping transfer tax exemption," "GST exemption," "marital deduction," "maximum marital deduction," "unlimited marital deduction," "inclusion ratio," "applicable fraction," or any section of the Internal Revenue Code relating to the federal estate tax or federal generation-skipping transfer tax, or that measures a share of an estate or trust by reference to federal estate taxes or federal generation-skipping transfer taxes, is deemed to refer to the federal estate tax and federal generation-skipping transfer tax laws as they applied with respect to the estates of decedents dying on December 31, 2009. This paragraph does not apply to a governing instrument, including a will or trust agreement, that manifests an intent that a contrary rule will apply if the decedent dies on a date on which there is no then-applicable federal estate or federal generation-skipping transfer tax.</p>
        <p>(b) The personal representative, trustee, or any interested person under the governing instrument, including a will or trust agreement, may bring a proceeding to determine whether the decedent intended that a formula or provision described in paragraph (a) be construed with respect to the law as it existed after December 31, 2009. This proceeding must be commenced by December 31, 2011, and the court may consider extrinsic evidence that contradicts the plain meaning of the will, trust, or other governing instrument. The court may modify a provision of a will, trust, or other governing instrument that refers to the federal estate tax or generation-skipping transfer tax laws as described in paragraph (a) to conform the terms to the decedent's intention, or achieve the decedent's tax objectives in a manner that is not contrary to the decedent's probable intention. The court may provide that its decision, including any decision to modify a provision of a will, trust, or other governing instrument, is effective as of the date of the decedent's death.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-712.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/334/#laws.0.14.0">2010 c 334 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/66/#laws.0.3.0">2011 c 66 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 8<br>GENERAL PROVISIONS CONCERNING<br>PROBATE AND NONPROBATE <br>TRANSFERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-802">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-802 EFFECT OF DISSOLUTION OF MARRIAGE, ANNULMENT, AND DECREE OF SEPARATION.</h1>
        <p>A person whose marriage to the decedent has been dissolved or annulled is not a surviving spouse unless, by virtue of a subsequent marriage, the person is married to the decedent at the time of death. A decree of separation which does not terminate the status of spouses is not a dissolution of marriage for purposes of this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-802.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; 1986 c 444; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/101/#laws.3.2.0">2024 c 101 art 3 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-803">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-803 EFFECT OF HOMICIDE ON INTESTATE SUCCESSION, WILLS, JOINT ASSETS, LIFE INSURANCE AND BENEFICIARY DESIGNATIONS; EMERGENCY ORDER.</h1>
        <p>(a) A surviving spouse, heir or devisee who feloniously and intentionally kills the decedent is not entitled to any benefits under the will or under this article, including an intestate share, an elective share, an omitted spouse's or child's share, homestead, exempt property, and a family allowance, and the estate of decedent passes as if the killer had predeceased the decedent. Property appointed by the will of the decedent to or for the benefit of the killer passes as if the killer had predeceased the decedent.</p>
        <p>(b) Any joint tenant who feloniously and intentionally kills another joint tenant thereby effects a severance of the interest of the decedent so that the share of the decedent passes as the decedent's property and the killer has no rights by survivorship. This provision applies to joint tenancies in real and personal property, joint accounts in banks, savings associations, credit unions and other institutions, and any other form of co-ownership with survivorship incidents.</p>
        <p>(c) A named beneficiary of a bond or other contractual arrangement who feloniously and intentionally kills the principal obligee is not entitled to any benefit under the bond or other contractual arrangement and it becomes payable as though the killer had predeceased the decedent.</p>
        <p>(d) A named beneficiary of a life insurance policy who feloniously and intentionally kills the person upon whose life the policy is issued is not entitled to any benefit under the policy and the proceeds of the policy shall be paid and distributed by order of the court as hereinafter provided. If a person who feloniously and intentionally kills a person upon whose life a life insurance policy is issued is a beneficial owner as shareholder, partner or beneficiary of a corporation, partnership, trust or association which is the named beneficiary of the life insurance policy, to the extent of the killer's beneficial ownership of the corporation, partnership, trust or association, the proceeds of the policy shall be paid and distributed by order of the court as hereinafter provided.</p>
        <p>Upon receipt of written notice by the insurance company at its home office that the insured may have been intentionally and feloniously killed by one or more named beneficiaries or that the insured may have been intentionally and feloniously killed by one or more persons who have a beneficial ownership in a corporation, partnership, trust or association, which is the named beneficiary of the life insurance policy, the insurance company shall, pending court order, withhold payment of the policy proceeds to all beneficiaries. In the event that the notice has not been received by the insurance company before payment of the policy proceeds, the insurance company shall be fully and finally discharged and released from any and all responsibility under the policy to the extent that the policy proceeds have been paid.</p>
        <p>The named beneficiary, the insurance company or any other party claiming an interest in the policy proceeds may commence an action in the district court to compel payment of the policy proceeds. The court may order the insurance company to pay the policy proceeds to any person equitably entitled thereto, including the deceased insured's spouse, children, issue, parents, creditors or estate, and may order the insurance company to pay the proceeds of the policy to the court pending the final determination of distribution of the proceeds by the court. The insurance company, upon receipt of a court order, judgment or decree ordering payment of the policy proceeds, shall pay the policy proceeds according to the terms of the order, and upon payment of such proceeds according to the terms of the court order, shall be fully and completely discharged and released from any and all responsibility for payment under the policy.</p>
        <p>(e) Any other acquisition of property or interest by the killer shall be treated in accordance with the principles of this section.</p>
        <p>(f) A final judgment of conviction of felonious and intentional killing is conclusive for purposes of this section. In the absence of a conviction of felonious and intentional killing the court may determine by a preponderance of evidence whether the killing was felonious and intentional for purposes of this section.</p>
        <p>(g) This section does not affect the rights of any person who, before rights under this section have been adjudicated, purchases from the killer for value and without notice property which the killer would have acquired except for this section, but the killer is liable for the amount of the proceeds or the value of the property. Any insurance company, bank, or other obligor making payment according to the terms of its policy or obligation is not liable by reason of this section unless prior to payment it has received at its home office or principal address written notice of a claim under this section.</p>
        <p>(h) If a complaint or indictment is issued charging a defendant in the felonious and intentional killing of the decedent, the personal representative, special administrator, or an interested person may file with the court a copy of the complaint or indictment and an inventory of the decedent's personal property that may be affected by a determination under this section, including the following:</p>
        <p>(1) personal property that is the subject of a specific devise under the decedent's will or separate writing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-513">524.2-513</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) exempt property identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-403">524.2-403</a>;</p>
        <p>(3) personal property claimed to have sentimental value to an eligible child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/525.152">525.152</a>; or</p>
        <p>(4) any other personal property believed, in good faith, to belong to the decedent.</p>
        <p class="bl">The person filing the inventory shall attest that the inventory is correct and complete to the best of the person's knowledge. The inventory and affidavit shall be filed with the court administrator.</p>
        <p>(i) Upon motion of the personal representative, special administrator, or an interested person, and for good cause shown, the court may issue an order regarding property identified under paragraph (h) that includes any or all of the following relief:</p>
        <p>(1) reserving a determination of distribution of property;</p>
        <p>(2) holding the property in trust or escrow for any and all beneficiaries or heirs not disqualified by this section;</p>
        <p>(3) prohibiting the sale, disposition, removal, transfer, or destruction of the property;</p>
        <p>(4) permitting the sale, disposition, removal, or transfer of property jointly held by the decedent and a person disqualified under this section only to the extent necessary for reasonable and ordinary living expenses by the disqualified individual, subject to approval and on the terms the court deems just and proper; or</p>
        <p>(5) any other relief prescribed by the court.</p>
        <p class="bl">An order may be granted without notice to other interested persons if the court finds from affidavit or other sworn testimony that the rights of decedent's heirs and beneficiaries may be irreparably harmed before a hearing can be held. If an ex parte order is granted, notice must be given to interested persons known by the court and as the court directs within 48 hours of issuing the order and the court shall hold a hearing within five days of issuing the order. In all other cases, reasonable notice of the time and place of hearing on the petition must be given to interested persons known by the court and as the court directs. An order issued under this paragraph shall be effective pending a final determination under this section unless the court specifies a shorter time period or rescinds the order.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-803.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/347/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 347 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/315/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 315 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/202/#laws.1.25.0">1995 c 202 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/338/#laws.2.3.0">1996 c 338 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/94/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 94 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-804">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-804 REVOCATION BY DISSOLUTION OF MARRIAGE; NO REVOCATION BY OTHER CHANGES OF CIRCUMSTANCES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-804.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-804.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Revocation upon dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided by the express terms of a governing instrument, other than a trust instrument under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501C.1207">501C.1207</a>, executed prior to the dissolution or annulment of an individual's marriage, a court order, a contract relating to the division of the marital property made between individuals before or after their marriage, dissolution, or annulment, or a plan document governing a qualified or nonqualified retirement plan, the dissolution or annulment of a marriage revokes any revocable:</p>
          <p>(1) disposition, beneficiary designation, or appointment of property made by an individual to the individual's former spouse in a governing instrument;</p>
          <p>(2) provision in a governing instrument conferring a general or nongeneral power of appointment on an individual's former spouse; and</p>
          <p>(3) nomination in a governing instrument, nominating an individual's former spouse to serve in any fiduciary or representative capacity, including a personal representative, executor, trustee, conservator, agent, or guardian.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-804.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-804.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effect of revocation.</span></h2>
          <p>Provisions of a governing instrument are given effect as if the former spouse died immediately before the dissolution or annulment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-804.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-804.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Revival if dissolution nullified.</span></h2>
          <p>Provisions revoked solely by this section are revived by the individual's remarriage to the former spouse or by a nullification of the dissolution or annulment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-804.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-804.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">No revocation for other change of circumstances.</span></h2>
          <p>No change of circumstances other than as described in this section and in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-803">524.2-803</a> effects a revocation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-804.5">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-804.5">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Protection of payors and other third parties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A payor or other third party is not liable for having made a payment or transferred an item of property or any other benefit to a beneficiary designated in a governing instrument affected by a dissolution, annulment, or remarriage, or for having taken any other action in good faith reliance on the validity of the governing instrument, before the payor or other third party received written notice of the dissolution, annulment, or remarriage. A payor or other third party is liable for a payment made or other action taken after the payor or other third party received written notice of a claimed forfeiture or revocation under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) Written notice of the dissolution, annulment, or remarriage under paragraph (a) must be delivered to the payor's or other third party's main office or home. Upon receipt of written notice of the dissolution, annulment, or remarriage, a payor or other third party may pay any amount owed or transfer or deposit any item of property held by it to or with the court having jurisdiction of the probate proceedings relating to the decedent's estate or, if no proceedings have been commenced, to or with the court having jurisdiction of probate proceedings relating to decedents' estates located in the county of the decedent's residence. The court shall hold the funds or item of property and, upon its determination under this section, shall order disbursement or transfer in accordance with the determination. Payments, transfers, or deposits made to or with the court discharge the payor or other third party from all claims for the value of amounts paid to or items of property transferred to or deposited with the court.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-804.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/130/#laws.0.13.0">1995 c 130 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/347/#laws.0.2.0">2002 c 347 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/5/#laws.15.13.0">2015 c 5 art 15 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-805">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-805 REFORMATION TO CORRECT MISTAKES.</h1>
        <p>The court may reform the terms of a governing instrument, even if unambiguous, to conform the terms to the transferor's intention, if it is proved by clear and convincing evidence what the transferor's intention was and that the terms of the governing instrument were affected by a mistake of a fact or law, whether in expression or inducement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-805.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/135/#laws.2.28.0">2016 c 135 art 2 s 28</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-806">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-806 MODIFICATION TO ACHIEVE TRANSFEROR'S TAX OBJECTIVES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-806.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-806.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Permitted purposes.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to achieve tax objectives that are clearly provided for in the transferor's will, the court may modify the terms of a governing instrument, in a manner that is not contrary to the transferor's probable intention, to ensure that the governing instrument correctly creates an interest:</p>
          <p>(1) in which a surviving spouse has a qualifying income interest with respect to which an election has been or will be made in whole or in part under section 2056(b)(7), 2056A, or 2523(f) of the Internal Revenue Code;</p>
          <p>(2) which will qualify for the marital deduction under section 2056 or 2056A of the Internal Revenue Code, by election or otherwise;</p>
          <p>(3) which will qualify for the charitable deduction under section 2055, 2522, or 642(c) of the Internal Revenue Code;</p>
          <p>(4) that is to be excepted, excluded, or exempt from or under chapter 13 (tax on generation skipping transfers) of the Internal Revenue Code; or</p>
          <p>(5) in a trust that satisfies the criteria for qualified subchapter S trusts under section 1361(d) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-806.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-806.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">May be retroactive.</span></h2>
          <p>The court may provide that a modification under this section has retroactive effect.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-806.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/135/#laws.2.29.0">2016 c 135 art 2 s 29</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 10<br>INTERNATIONAL WILL<br>INFORMATION REGISTRATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1001">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1001 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1001.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1001.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1001">524.2-1001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1010">524.2-1010</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings ascribed to them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1001.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1001.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">International will.</span></h2>
          <p>"International will" means a will executed in conformity with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1002">524.2-1002</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1005">524.2-1005</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1001.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1001.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Authorized person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Authorized person" and "person authorized to act in connection with international wills" means a person who by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1009">524.2-1009</a>, or by the laws of the United States including members of the diplomatic and consular service of the United States designated by Foreign Service Regulations, is empowered to supervise the execution of international wills.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1001.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.2.0">1978 c 525 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1002">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1002 INTERNATIONAL WILL; VALIDITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1002.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1002.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Form.</span></h2>
          <p>A will is valid as regards form, irrespective particularly of the place where it is made, of the location of the assets and of the nationality, domicile, or residence of the testator, if it is made in the form of an international will complying with the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1002">524.2-1002</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1005">524.2-1005</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1002.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1002.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effect of invalidity.</span></h2>
          <p>The invalidity of the will as an international will does not affect its formal validity as a will of another kind.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1002.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1002.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Multiple testators.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1001">524.2-1001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1010">524.2-1010</a> do not apply to the form of testamentary dispositions made by two or more persons in one instrument.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1002.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.3.0">1978 c 525 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1003">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1003 INTERNATIONAL WILL; REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1003.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1003.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">In writing.</span></h2>
          <p>The will must be made in writing. It need not be written by the testator personally. It may be written in any language, by hand or by any other means.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1003.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1003.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Testator's declaration.</span></h2>
          <p>The testator shall declare in the presence of two witnesses and of a person authorized to act in connection with international wills that the document is the testator's will and that the testator knows the contents thereof. The testator need not inform the witnesses or the authorized person of the contents of the will.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1003.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1003.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Signature.</span></h2>
          <p>In the presence of the witnesses and of the authorized person, the testator shall sign the will or, having previously signed it, shall acknowledge the signature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1003.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1003.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Inability to sign.</span></h2>
          <p>If the testator is unable to sign, the absence of the signature does not affect the validity of the international will if the testator indicates the reason for the inability to sign and the authorized person makes note thereof on the will. In that case, it is permissible for any other person present, including the authorized person or one of the witnesses, at the direction of the testator, to sign the testator's name for the testator if the authorized person makes note of this on the will, but it is not required that any person sign the testator's name for the testator.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1003.5">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1003.5">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Attestation.</span></h2>
          <p>The witnesses and the authorized person shall there and then attest the will by signing in the presence of the testator.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1003.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.4.0">1978 c 525 s 4</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1004">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1004 INTERNATIONAL WILLS; OTHER POINTS OF FORM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1004.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1004.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Multiple pages.</span></h2>
          <p>The signatures must be placed at the end of the will. If the will consists of several sheets, each sheet must be signed by the testator or, if the testator is unable to sign, by the person signing on the testator's behalf or, if there is no such person, by the authorized person. In addition, each sheet must be numbered.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1004.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1004.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Date.</span></h2>
          <p>The date of the will must be the date of its signature by the authorized person. That date must be noted at the end of the will by the authorized person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1004.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1004.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Safekeeping.</span></h2>
          <p>The authorized person shall ask whether the testator wishes to make a declaration concerning the safekeeping of the will. If so and at the express request of the testator, the place where the testator intends to have the will kept must be mentioned in the certificate provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1005">524.2-1005</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1004.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1004.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Validity.</span></h2>
          <p>A will executed in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1003">524.2-1003</a> is not invalid merely because it does not comply with this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1004.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.5.0">1978 c 525 s 5</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1005">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1005 INTERNATIONAL WILL; CERTIFICATE.</h1>
        <p>The authorized person shall sign and attach to the will a certificate establishing that the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1002">524.2-1002</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1005">524.2-1005</a> for valid execution of an international will have been fulfilled. The authorized person shall keep a copy of the certificate and deliver another to the testator. The certificate must be substantially in the following form:</p>
        <p class="ce">CERTIFICATE</p>
        <p class="ce1">(Convention of October 26, 1973)</p>
        <p>1. I, .............. (name, address, and capacity),</p>
        <p>a person authorized to act in connection with</p>
        <p>international wills,</p>
        <p>2. certify that on ....... (date)</p>
        <p>at..................(place)</p>
        <p>3. (testator) .............................(name,</p>
        <p>address, date and place of birth) in my</p>
        <p>presence and that of the witnesses</p>
        <p>4. (a) ...............(name, address, date and</p>
        <p>place of birth)</p>
        <p>(b) ...............(name, address, date and</p>
        <p>place of birth)</p>
        <p>has declared that the attached document is his/her will</p>
        <p>and that ..he knows the contents thereof.</p>
        <p>5. I furthermore certify that:</p>
        <p>6. (a) in my presence and in that of the witnesses</p>
        <p>(1) the testator has signed the will or has</p>
        <p>acknowledged his/her signature previously affixed.</p>
        <p>*(2) following a declaration of the testator stating</p>
        <p>that the testator was unable to sign the will</p>
        <p>for the following</p>
        <p>reason.................................,</p>
        <p>I have mentioned this declaration on the will,</p>
        <p>*and the signature has been affixed</p>
        <p>by.....................</p>
        <p>(name and address)</p>
        <p>7. (b) the witnesses and I have signed the will;</p>
        <p>8. *(c) each page of the will has been signed</p>
        <p>by ................ and numbered;</p>
        <p>9. (d) I have satisfied myself as to the identity</p>
        <p>of the testator and of the witnesses as</p>
        <p>designated above;</p>
        <p>10. (e) the witnesses met the conditions requisite</p>
        <p>to act as such according to the law under which</p>
        <p>I am acting;</p>
        <p>11. *(f) the testator has requested me to include</p>
        <p>the following</p>
        <p>statement concerning the safekeeping of the will:</p>
        <p>....................................................</p>
        <p>12. ..............................PLACE OF EXECUTION</p>
        <p>13. ..............................DATE</p>
        <p>14. ..............................SIGNATURE</p>
        <p>and, if necessary, SEAL........</p>
        <p>*to be completed if appropriate</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1005.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.6.0">1978 c 525 s 6</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1006">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1006 INTERNATIONAL WILL; EFFECT OF CERTIFICATE.</h1>
        <p>In the absence of evidence to the contrary, the certificate of the authorized person is conclusive of the formal validity of the instrument as a will under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1001">524.2-1001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1010">524.2-1010</a>. The absence or irregularity of a certificate does not affect the formal validity of a will under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1001">524.2-1001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1010">524.2-1010</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1006.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.7.0">1978 c 525 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1007">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1007 INTERNATIONAL WILL; REVOCATION.</h1>
        <p>An international will is subject to the ordinary rules of revocation of wills.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1007.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.8.0">1978 c 525 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1008">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1008 SOURCE AND CONSTRUCTION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1001">524.2-1001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1007">524.2-1007</a> derive from Annex to Convention of October 26, 1973, Providing a Uniform Law on the Form of an International Will. In interpreting and applying sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1001">524.2-1001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1007">524.2-1007</a>, regard shall be had to its international origin and to the need for uniformity in its interpretation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1008.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.9.0">1978 c 525 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1009">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1009 PERSONS AUTHORIZED TO ACT IN RELATION TO INTERNATIONAL WILL; ELIGIBILITY; RECOGNITION BY AUTHORIZING AGENCY.</h1>
        <p>Individuals who have been admitted to practice law before the courts of this state and are currently licensed so to do are authorized persons in relation to international wills.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1009.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.10.0">1978 c 525 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1010">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1010 INTERNATIONAL WILL INFORMATION REGISTRATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1010.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1010.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Registry.</span></h2>
          <p>The secretary of state shall establish a registry system by which authorized persons may register, in a central information center, information regarding the execution of international wills. The information shall be private until the death of the testator, after which date it shall be available to any person desiring information about any will who presents a death record or other satisfactory evidence of the testator's death to the secretary of state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1010.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1010.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transmission to other registry.</span></h2>
          <p>The secretary of state, at the request of the authorized person, may cause the information received about execution of any international will to be transmitted to the registry system of another jurisdiction as identified by the testator, if that other system adheres to rules protecting the confidentiality of the information similar to those established in this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.2-1010.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.2-1010.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Information to be registered.</span></h2>
          <p>Only the following information may be received, preserved and reported pursuant to this section:</p>
          <p>(a) The testator's name, Social Security number or other individual identifying number established by law;</p>
          <p>(b) The testator's address and date and place of birth; and</p>
          <p>(c) The intended place of deposit or safekeeping of the instrument pending the death of the testator.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1010.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/525/#laws.0.11.0">1978 c 525 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.15.32.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 15 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Part 11<br>UNIFORM DISCLAIMER OF <br>PROPERTY INTERESTS ACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1101">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1101 SHORT TITLE.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1101">524.2-1101</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.2-1116">524.2-1116</a> may be cited as the "Uniform Disclaimer of Property Interests Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.2-1101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/67/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 67 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.2-1102">
        <h1 class="shn">524.2-1102 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
