        <p>(b) Courts may communicate concerning schedules, calendars, court records, and other administrative matters without making a record.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-604.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.5.0">2009 c 46 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-605">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-605 COOPERATION BETWEEN COURTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) In a guardianship or protective proceeding in this state, a court of this state may request the appropriate court of another state to do any one or more of the following:</p>
        <p>(1) hold an evidentiary hearing;</p>
        <p>(2) order a person in that state to produce evidence or give testimony pursuant to procedures of that state;</p>
        <p>(3) order that an evaluation or assessment be made of the respondent;</p>
        <p>(4) order any appropriate investigation of a person involved in a proceeding;</p>
        <p>(5) forward to the court of this state a certified copy of the transcript or other record of a hearing under clause (1) or any other proceeding, any evidence otherwise produced under clause (2), and any evaluation or assessment prepared in compliance with an order under clause (3) or (4);</p>
        <p>(6) issue any order necessary to assure the appearance in the proceeding of a person whose presence is necessary for the court to make a determination, including the respondent or the incapacitated or protected person; and</p>
        <p>(7) issue an order authorizing the release of medical, financial, criminal, or other relevant information in that state, including protected health information as defined in Code of Federal Regulations, title 45, section 164.504.</p>
        <p>(b) If a court of another state in which a guardianship or protective proceeding is pending requests assistance of the kind provided in paragraph (a), a court of this state has jurisdiction for the limited purpose of granting the request or making reasonable efforts to comply with the request.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-605.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.6.0">2009 c 46 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-606">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-606 TAKING TESTIMONY IN ANOTHER STATE.</h1>
        <p>(a) In a guardianship or protective proceeding, in addition to other procedures that may be available, testimony of a witness who is located in another state may be offered by deposition or other means allowable in this state for testimony taken in another state. The court on its own motion may order that the testimony of a witness be taken in another state and may prescribe the manner in which and the terms upon which the testimony is to be taken.</p>
        <p>(b) In a guardianship or protective proceeding, a court in this state may permit a witness located in another state to be deposed or to testify by telephone or audiovisual or other electronic means. A court of this state shall cooperate with the court of the other state in designating an appropriate location for the deposition or testimony.</p>
        <p>(c) Documentary evidence transmitted from another state to a court of this state by technological means that do not produce an original writing may not be excluded from evidence on an objection based on the best evidence rule.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-606.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.7.0">2009 c 46 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-701">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-701 DEFINITIONS; SIGNIFICANT CONNECTION FACTORS.</h1>
        <p>(a) In sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-701">524.5-701</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-709">524.5-709</a>:</p>
        <p>(1) "emergency" means a circumstance that likely will result in substantial harm to a respondent's health, safety, or welfare, and for which the appointment of a guardian is necessary because no other person has authority and is willing to act on the respondent's behalf;</p>
        <p>(2) "home state" means the state in which the respondent was physically present, including any period of temporary absence, for at least six consecutive months immediately before the filing of a petition for a protective order or the appointment of a guardian; or if none, the state in which the respondent was physically present, including any period of temporary absence, for at least six consecutive months ending within the six months prior to the filing of the petition; and</p>
        <p>(3) "significant-connection state" means a state, other than the home state, with which a respondent has a significant connection other than mere physical presence and in which substantial evidence concerning the respondent is available.</p>
        <p>(b) In determining under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-703">524.5-703 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-801">524.5-801</a>, paragraph (e), whether a respondent has a significant connection with a particular state, the court shall consider:</p>
        <p>(1) the location of the respondent's family and other persons required to be notified of the guardianship or protective proceeding;</p>
        <p>(2) the length of time the respondent at any time was physically present in the state and the duration of any absence;</p>
        <p>(3) the location of the respondent's property; and</p>
        <p>(4) the extent to which the respondent has ties to the state such as voting registration, state or local tax return filing, vehicle registration, driver's license, social relationship, and receipt of services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-701.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.8.0">2009 c 46 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.79.0">2010 c 382 s 79</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-702">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-702 EXCLUSIVE BASIS.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-701">524.5-701</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-709">524.5-709</a> provide the exclusive jurisdictional basis for a court of this state to appoint a guardian or issue a protective order for an adult.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-702.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.9.0">2009 c 46 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-703">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-703 JURISDICTION.</h1>
        <p>A court of this state has jurisdiction to appoint a guardian or issue a protective order for a respondent if:</p>
        <p>(1) this state is the respondent's home state;</p>
        <p>(2) on the date the petition is filed, this state is a significant-connection state and:</p>
        <p>(i) the respondent does not have a home state or a court of the respondent's home state has declined to exercise jurisdiction because this state is a more appropriate forum; or</p>
        <p>(ii) the respondent has a home state, a petition for an appointment or order is not pending in a court of that state or another significant-connection state, and, before the court makes the appointment or issues the order:</p>
        <p>(A) a petition for an appointment or order is not filed in the respondent's home state;</p>
        <p>(B) an objection to the court's jurisdiction is not filed by a person required to be notified of the proceeding; and</p>
        <p>(C) the court in this state concludes that it is an appropriate forum under the factors set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-706">524.5-706</a>;</p>
        <p>(3) this state does not have jurisdiction under either clause (1) or (2), the respondent's home state and all significant-connection states have declined to exercise jurisdiction because this state is the more appropriate forum, and jurisdiction in this state is consistent with the constitutions of this state and the United States; or</p>
        <p>(4) the requirements for special jurisdiction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-704">524.5-704</a> are met.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-703.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.10.0">2009 c 46 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-704">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-704 SPECIAL JURISDICTION.</h1>
        <p>(a) A court of this state lacking jurisdiction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-703">524.5-703</a> has special jurisdiction to do any of the following:</p>
        <p>(1) appoint a guardian in an emergency for a term not exceeding 90 days for a respondent who is physically present in this state;</p>
        <p>(2) issue a protective order with respect to real or tangible personal property located in this state; and</p>
        <p>(3) appoint a guardian or conservator for an incapacitated or protected person for whom a provisional order to transfer the proceeding from another state has been issued under procedures similar to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-801">524.5-801</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) If a petition for the appointment of a guardian in an emergency is brought in this state and this state was not the respondent's home state on the date the petition was filed, the court shall dismiss the proceeding at the request of the court of the home state, if any, whether dismissal is requested before or after the emergency appointment.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-704.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.11.0">2009 c 46 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-705">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-705 EXCLUSIVE AND CONTINUING JURISDICTION.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-704">524.5-704</a>, a court that has appointed a guardian or issued a protective order consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-601">524.5-601</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-903">524.5-903</a> has exclusive and continuing jurisdiction over the proceeding until it is terminated by the court or the appointment or order expires by its own terms.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-705.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.12.0">2009 c 46 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-706">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-706 APPROPRIATE FORUM.</h1>
        <p>(a) A court of this state having jurisdiction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-703">524.5-703</a> to appoint a guardian or issue a protective order may decline to exercise its jurisdiction if it determines at any time that a court of another state is a more appropriate forum.</p>
        <p>(b) If a court of this state declines to exercise its jurisdiction under paragraph (a), it shall either dismiss or stay the proceeding. The court may impose any condition the court considers just and proper, including the condition that a petition for the appointment of a guardian or issuance of a protective order be filed promptly in another state.</p>
        <p>(c) In determining whether it is an appropriate forum, the court shall consider all relevant factors, including:</p>
        <p>(1) any expressed preference of the respondent;</p>
        <p>(2) whether abuse, neglect, or exploitation of the respondent has occurred or is likely to occur and which state could best protect the respondent from the abuse, neglect, or exploitation;</p>
        <p>(3) the length of time the respondent was physically present in or was a legal resident of this or another state;</p>
        <p>(4) the distance of the respondent from the court in each state;</p>
        <p>(5) the financial circumstances of the respondent's estate;</p>
        <p>(6) the nature and location of the evidence;</p>
        <p>(7) the ability of the court in each state to decide the issue expeditiously and the procedures necessary to present evidence;</p>
        <p>(8) the familiarity of the court of each state with the facts and issues in the proceeding; and</p>
        <p>(9) if an appointment were made, the court's ability to monitor the conduct of the guardian or conservator.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-706.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.13.0">2009 c 46 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-707">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-707 JURISDICTION DECLINED BY REASON OF CONDUCT.</h1>
        <p>(a) If at any time a court of this state determines that it acquired jurisdiction to appoint a guardian or issue a protective order because of unjustifiable conduct, the court may:</p>
        <p>(1) decline to exercise jurisdiction;</p>
        <p>(2) exercise jurisdiction for the limited purpose of fashioning an appropriate remedy to ensure the health, safety, and welfare of the respondent or the protection of the respondent's property or prevent a repetition of the unjustifiable conduct, including staying the proceeding until a petition for the appointment of a guardian or issuance of a protective order is filed in a court of another state having jurisdiction; or</p>
        <p>(3) continue to exercise jurisdiction after considering:</p>
        <p>(i) the extent to which the respondent and all persons required to be notified of the proceedings have acquiesced in the exercise of the court's jurisdiction;</p>
        <p>(ii) whether it is a more appropriate forum than the court of any other state under the factors set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-706">524.5-706</a>, paragraph (c); and</p>
        <p>(iii) whether the court of any other state would have jurisdiction under factual circumstances in substantial conformity with the jurisdictional standards of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-703">524.5-703</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) If a court of this state determines that it acquired jurisdiction to appoint a guardian or issue a protective order because a party seeking to invoke its jurisdiction engaged in unjustifiable conduct, it may assess against that party necessary and reasonable expenses, including attorney fees, investigative fees, court costs, communication expenses, witness fees and expenses, and travel expenses. The court may not assess fees, costs, or expenses of any kind against this state or a governmental subdivision, agency, or instrumentality of this state unless authorized by law other than sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-601">524.5-601</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-903">524.5-903</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-707.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.14.0">2009 c 46 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-708">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-708 NOTICE OF PROCEEDING.</h1>
        <p>If a petition for the appointment of a guardian or issuance of a protective order is brought in this state and this state was not the respondent's home state on the date the petition was filed, in addition to complying with the notice requirements of this state, notice of the petition must be given to those persons who would be entitled to notice of the petition if a proceeding were brought in the respondent's home state. The notice must be given in the same manner as notice is required to be given in this state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-708.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.15.0">2009 c 46 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-709">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-709 PROCEEDINGS IN MORE THAN ONE STATE.</h1>
        <p>Except for a petition for the appointment of a guardian in an emergency or issuance of a protective order limited to property located in this state under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-704">524.5-704</a>, paragraph (a), clause (1) or (2), if a petition for the appointment of a guardian or issuance of a protective order is filed in this state and in another state and neither petition has been dismissed or withdrawn, the following rules apply:</p>
        <p>(1) If the court in this state has jurisdiction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-703">524.5-703</a>, it may proceed with the case unless a court in another state acquires jurisdiction under provisions similar to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-703">524.5-703</a> before the appointment or issuance of the order.</p>
        <p>(2) If the court in this state does not have jurisdiction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-703">524.5-703</a>, whether at the time the petition is filed or at any time before the appointment or issuance of the order, the court shall stay the proceeding and communicate with the court in the other state. If the court in the other state has jurisdiction, the court in this state shall dismiss the petition unless the court in the other state determines that the court in this state is a more appropriate forum.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-709.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.16.0">2009 c 46 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-801">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-801 TRANSFER OF GUARDIANSHIP OR CONSERVATORSHIP TO ANOTHER STATE.</h1>
        <p>(a) A guardian or conservator appointed in this state may petition the court to transfer the guardianship or conservatorship to another state.</p>
        <p>(b) Notice of a petition under paragraph (a) must be given to the persons that would be entitled to notice of a petition in this state for the appointment of a guardian or conservator.</p>
        <p>(c) On the court's own motion or on request of the guardian or conservator, the incapacitated or protected person, or other person required to be notified of the petition, the court shall hold a hearing on a petition filed pursuant to paragraph (a).</p>
        <p>(d) The court shall issue an order provisionally granting a petition to transfer a guardianship and shall direct the guardian to petition for guardianship in the other state if the court is satisfied that the guardianship will be accepted by the court in the other state and the court finds that:</p>
        <p>(1) the incapacitated person is physically present in or is reasonably expected to move permanently to the other state;</p>
        <p>(2) an objection to the transfer has not been made or, if an objection has been made, the objector has not established that the transfer would be contrary to the interests of the incapacitated person; and</p>
        <p>(3) plans for care and services for the incapacitated person in the other state are reasonable and sufficient.</p>
        <p>(e) The court shall issue a provisional order granting a petition to transfer a conservatorship and shall direct the conservator to petition for conservatorship in the other state if the court is satisfied that the conservatorship will be accepted by the court of the other state and the court finds that:</p>
        <p>(1) the protected person is physically present in or is reasonably expected to move permanently to the other state, or the protected person has a significant connection to the other state considering the factors in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-701">524.5-701</a>, paragraph (b);</p>
        <p>(2) an objection to the transfer has not been made or, if an objection has been made, the objector has not established that the transfer would be contrary to the interests of the protected person; and</p>
        <p>(3) adequate arrangements will be made for management of the protected person's property.</p>
        <p>(f) The court shall issue a final order confirming the transfer and terminating the guardianship or conservatorship upon its receipt of:</p>
        <p>(1) a provisional order accepting the proceeding from the court to which the proceeding is to be transferred which is issued under provisions similar to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-802">524.5-802</a>; and</p>
        <p>(2) the documents required to terminate a guardianship or conservatorship in this state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-801.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.17.0">2009 c 46 s 17</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-802">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-802 ACCEPTING GUARDIANSHIP OR CONSERVATORSHIP TRANSFERRED FROM ANOTHER STATE.</h1>
        <p>(a) To confirm transfer of a guardianship or conservatorship transferred to this state under provisions similar to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-801">524.5-801</a>, the guardian or conservator must petition the court in this state to accept the guardianship or conservatorship. The petition must include a certified copy of the other state's provisional order of transfer.</p>
        <p>(b) Notice of a petition under paragraph (a) must be given to those persons that would be entitled to notice if the petition were a petition for the appointment of a guardian or issuance of a protective order in both the transferring state and this state. The notice must be given in the same manner as notice is required to be given in this state.</p>
        <p>(c) On the court's own motion or on request of the guardian or conservator, the incapacitated or protected person, or other person required to be notified of the proceeding, the court shall hold a hearing on a petition filed pursuant to paragraph (a).</p>
        <p>(d) The court shall issue an order provisionally granting a petition filed under paragraph (a) unless:</p>
        <p>(1) an objection is made and the objector establishes that transfer of the proceeding would be contrary to the interests of the incapacitated or protected person; or</p>
        <p>(2) the guardian or conservator is ineligible for appointment in this state.</p>
        <p>(e) The court shall issue a final order accepting the proceeding and appointing the guardian or conservator as guardian or conservator in this state upon its receipt from the court from which the proceeding is being transferred of a final order issued under provisions similar to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-801">524.5-801</a> transferring the proceeding to this state.</p>
        <p>(f) Not later than 90 days after issuance of a final order accepting transfer of a guardianship or conservatorship, the court shall determine whether the guardianship or conservatorship needs to be modified to conform to the law of this state.</p>
        <p>(g) In granting a petition under this section, the court shall recognize a guardianship or conservatorship order from the other state, including the determination of the incapacitated or protected person's incapacity and the appointment of the guardian or conservator.</p>
        <p>(h) The denial by a court of this state of a petition to accept a guardianship or conservatorship transferred from another state does not affect the ability of the guardian or conservator to seek appointment as guardian or conservator in this state under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-101">524.5-101</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-502">524.5-502</a> if the court has jurisdiction to make an appointment other than by reason of the provisional order of transfer.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-802.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.18.0">2009 c 46 s 18</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-901">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-901 REGISTRATION OF GUARDIANSHIP ORDERS.</h1>
        <p>If a guardian has been appointed in another state and a petition for the appointment of a guardian is not pending in this state, the guardian appointed in the other state, after giving notice to the appointing court of an intent to register, may register the guardianship order in this state by filing as a foreign judgment in a court, in any appropriate county of this state, certified copies of the order and letters of office.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-901.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.19.0">2009 c 46 s 19</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-902">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-902 REGISTRATION OF PROTECTIVE ORDERS.</h1>
        <p>If a conservator has been appointed in another state and a petition for a protective order is not pending in this state, the conservator appointed in the other state, after giving notice to the appointing court of an intent to register, may register the protective order in this state by filing as a foreign judgment in a court of this state, in any county in which property belonging to the protected person is located, certified copies of the order and letters of office and of any bond.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-902.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.20.0">2009 c 46 s 20</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-903">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-903 EFFECT OF REGISTRATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Upon registration of a guardianship or protective order from another state, the guardian or conservator may exercise in this state all powers authorized in the order of appointment except as prohibited under the laws of this state, including maintaining actions and proceedings in this state and, if the guardian or conservator is not a resident of this state, subject to any conditions imposed upon nonresident parties.</p>
        <p>(b) A court of this state may grant relief available under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-601">524.5-601</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-903">524.5-903</a> and other law of this state to enforce a registered order.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-903.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/46/#laws.0.21.0">2009 c 46 s 21</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.5-2011">
        <h1 class="shn">524.5-2011 COMPLIANCE WITH FEDERAL INDIAN CHILD WELFARE ACT AND MINNESOTA INDIAN FAMILY PRESERVATION ACT.</h1>
        <p>Proceedings under this chapter concerning an Indian child are child custody proceedings governed by the Indian Child Welfare Act, United States Code, title 25, sections 1901 to 1963; by the Minnesota Indian Family Preservation Act, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.751">260.751</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.835">260.835</a>; and by this chapter when not inconsistent with the federal Indian Child Welfare Act or the Minnesota Indian Family Preservation Act.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.5-2011.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.17.55.0">2024 c 115 art 17 s 55</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">Article 6<br>NONPROBATE TRANSFERS ON DEATH (1989)<br>Part 2<br>MINNESOTA MULTIPARTY ACCOUNTS ACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-201">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-201 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-201">524.6-201</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-214">524.6-214</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Account.</span></h2>
          <p>"Account" means a contract of deposit of funds between a depositor and a financial institution, and includes a checking account, savings account, certificate of deposit, share account and other like arrangement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.2a">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2a." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.2a">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Agent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agent" means a person authorized to make account transactions for a party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Financial institution.</span></h2>
          <p>"Financial institution" means any organization authorized to do business under state or federal laws relating to financial institutions, including, without limitation, banks and trust companies, savings banks, savings associations, and credit unions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Joint account.</span></h2>
          <p>"Joint account" means an account so designated, and any account payable on request to one or more of two or more parties and to the survivor of them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.5">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.5">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Multiple-party account.</span></h2>
          <p>A "multiple-party account" means a joint account or a P.O.D. account. It does not include accounts established for deposit of funds of a partnership, joint venture, or other association for business purposes, or accounts controlled by one or more persons as the duly authorized agent or trustee for a person, corporation, unincorporated association, charitable or civic organization or a regular fiduciary or trust account where the relationship is established other than by deposit agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.6">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 6." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.6">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Net contribution.</span></h2>
          <p>"Net contribution" of a party to a joint account as of any given time is the sum of all deposits thereto made by or for the party, less all withdrawals made by or for the party which have not been paid to or applied to the use of any other party, plus a pro rata share of any interest or dividends included in the current balance. The term includes any proceeds of deposit life insurance added to the account by reason of the death of the party whose net contribution is in question.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.7">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 7." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.7">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Party.</span></h2>
          <p>"Party" means a person who, by the terms of the account, has a present right, subject to request, to payment from a multiple-party account other than as an agent. A P.O.D. payee is a party only after the account becomes payable by reason of the payee surviving the original party. Unless the context otherwise requires, it includes a guardian, conservator, personal representative, or assignee, including an attaching creditor, of a party. It also includes a person identified as a trustee of an account for another whether or not a beneficiary is named, but it does not include any named beneficiary unless the beneficiary has a present right of withdrawal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.8">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 8." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.8">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Payment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Payment" of sums on deposit includes withdrawal, payment on check or other directive of a party, and any pledge of sums on deposit by a party and any setoff, or reduction or other disposition of all or part of an account pursuant to a pledge.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.9">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 9." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.9">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Proof of death.</span></h2>
          <p>"Proof of death" includes (a) a certified or authenticated copy of a death record purporting to be issued by an official or agency of the place where the death purportedly occurred which shall be prima facie proof of the fact, place, date and time of death and the identity of the decedent, (b) a certified or authenticated copy of any record or report of any governmental agency, domestic or foreign, that a person is dead which shall be prima facie evidence of the fact, place, date and time of death and the identity of the decedent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.10">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 10." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.10">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">P.O.D. account.</span></h2>
          <p>"P.O.D. account" means an account payable on request to one or more parties and on the death of the parties to one or more P.O.D. payees. The term also means an account in the name of one or more parties as trustee for one or more beneficiaries where the relationship is established by the form of the account and the deposit agreement with the financial institution and there is no subject of the trust other than the sums on deposit in the account. A P.O.D. account does not include a trust account established under a testamentary trust or inter vivos trust, or a fiduciary account arising from a fiduciary relationship such as attorney-client.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.11">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 11." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.11">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">P.O.D. payee.</span></h2>
          <p>"P.O.D. payee" means a person designated on a P.O.D. account as one to whom the account is payable on request after the death of one or more persons.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.12">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 12." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.12">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Request.</span></h2>
          <p>"Request" means a proper request for withdrawals, or a check or order for payment, which complies with all conditions of the account, including special requirements concerning necessary signatures and regulations of the financial institution; but if the financial institution conditions withdrawal or payment on advance notice, for purposes of this part the request for withdrawal or payment is treated as immediately effective and a notice of intent to withdraw is treated as a request for withdrawal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.13">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 13." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.13">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Sums on deposit.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sums on deposit" means the balance payable on a multiple-party account including interest, dividends and, in addition, any deposit life insurance proceeds added to the account by reason of the death of a party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-201.14">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 14." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-201.14">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Withdrawal.</span></h2>
          <p>"Withdrawal" includes payment to a third person pursuant to check or other directive of a party.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-201.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 619 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.9.0">1985 c 292 s 9</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.1.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/202/#laws.1.25.0">1995 c 202 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.15.32.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 15 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/36/#laws.0.2.0">2013 c 36 s 2</a>,3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-202">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-202 OWNERSHIP AS BETWEEN PARTIES, AND OTHERS; PROTECTION OF FINANCIAL INSTITUTIONS.</h1>
        <p>The provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-203">524.6-203</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-205">524.6-205</a> concerning beneficial ownership as between parties, or as between parties and P.O.D. payees or beneficiaries of multiple-party accounts, are relevant only to controversies between these persons and their creditors and other successors, and have no bearing on the power of withdrawal of these persons as determined by the terms of account contracts. The provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-208">524.6-208</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-212">524.6-212</a> govern the liability of financial institutions who make payments pursuant thereto, and their setoff rights.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-202.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 619 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-203">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-203 OWNERSHIP DURING LIFETIME.</h1>
        <p>(a) A joint account belongs, during the lifetime of all parties, to the parties in proportion to the net contributions by each to the sums on deposit, unless there is clear and convincing evidence of a different intent.</p>
        <p>(b) A P.O.D. account belongs to the original purchasing or depositing party during the party's lifetime and not to the P.O.D. payee or payees; if two or more parties are named as original parties, during their lifetimes, rights as between them are governed by paragraph (a).</p>
        <p>(c) An agent in an account with an agency designation has no beneficial right to sums on deposit by virtue of being named as an agent.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-203.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.4.0">1973 c 619 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.12.0">1985 c 292 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/36/#laws.0.4.0">2013 c 36 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-204">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-204 RIGHT OF SURVIVORSHIP.</h1>
        <p>(a) Sums remaining on deposit at the death of a party to a joint account belong to the surviving party or parties as against the estate of the decedent unless: (1) there is clear and convincing evidence of a different intention; or (2) there is a different disposition made by a valid will specifically referring to such account, as provided in this section. If there are two or more surviving parties, their respective ownerships during lifetime shall be in proportion to their previous ownership interests under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-203">524.6-203 </a>augmented by an equal share for each survivor of any interest the decedent may have owned in the account immediately before death; and the right of survivorship continues between the surviving parties. The interest so determined is also the interest disposable by will.</p>
        <p>(b) If the account is a P.O.D. account, on the death of the original party or of the survivor of two or more original parties, any sums remaining on deposit belong to the P.O.D. payees if surviving, or to the survivor of them if one or more die before the surviving original party; if two or more P.O.D. payees survive, there is no right of survivorship in event of death of a P.O.D. payee thereafter unless the terms of the account or deposit agreement expressly provide for survivorship between them.</p>
        <p>(c) In other cases, the death of any party to a multiple-party account has no effect on beneficial ownership of the account other than to transfer the rights of the decedent as part of the estate.</p>
        <p>(d) A right of survivorship arising from the express terms of the account, or under this section, or under a P.O.D. payee designation, may be changed by specific reference by will, but the terms of such will shall not be binding upon any financial institution unless it has been given a notice in writing of a claim thereunder, in which event the deposit shall remain undisbursed until an order has been made by the probate court adjudicating the decedent's interest disposable by will.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-204.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.5.0">1973 c 619 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.13.0">1985 c 292 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/36/#laws.0.5.0">2013 c 36 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-205">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-205 EFFECT OF A WRITTEN NOTICE TO FINANCIAL INSTITUTION.</h1>
        <p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-204">524.6-204</a> as to rights of survivorship are determined by the form of the account at the death of a party. This form may be altered by written order given by a party to the financial institution to change the form of the account or to stop or vary payment under the terms of the account. The order or request must be signed by a party and received by the financial institution during the party's lifetime.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-205.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.6.0">1973 c 619 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.14.0">1985 c 292 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-206">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-206 ACCOUNTS AND TRANSFERS NONTESTAMENTARY.</h1>
        <p>Any transfers resulting from the application of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-204">524.6-204</a> are effective by reason of the account contracts involved and this statute, and are not to be considered as subject to probate except as to the transfers expressly changed by will, as provided for by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-204">524.6-204</a>, clause (d).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-206.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.7.0">1973 c 619 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.15.0">1985 c 292 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-207">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-207 RIGHTS OF CREDITORS.</h1>
        <p>No multiple-party account will be effective against an estate of a deceased party to transfer to a survivor sums needed to pay debts, taxes, and expenses of administration, including statutory allowances to the surviving spouse, minor children and dependent children or against the state or a county agency with a claim authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.15">256B.15</a>, if other assets of the estate are insufficient, to the extent the deceased party is the source of the funds or beneficial owner. A surviving party or P.O.D. payee who receives payment from a multiple-party account after the death of a deceased party shall be liable to account to the deceased party's personal representative or the state or a county agency with a claim authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.15">256B.15</a> for amounts the decedent owned beneficially immediately before death to the extent necessary to discharge any such claims and charges remaining unpaid after the application of the assets of the decedent's estate. No proceeding to assert this liability shall be commenced by the personal representative unless the personal representative has received a written demand by a surviving spouse, a creditor or one acting for a minor dependent child of the decedent, and no proceeding shall be commenced later than two years following the death of the decedent. Sums recovered by the personal representative shall be administered as part of the decedent's estate. This section shall not affect the right of a financial institution to make payment on multiple-party accounts according to the terms thereof, or make it liable to the estate of a deceased party unless, before payment, the institution has been served with process in a proceeding by the personal representative or the state or a county agency with a claim authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.15">256B.15</a>, or has been presented by the state or a county agency with a claim authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.15">256B.15</a> with an affidavit pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.3-1201">524.3-1201</a>. Upon being presented with such an affidavit, the financial institution shall make payment of the multiple-party account to the affiant in an amount equal to the lesser of the claim stated in the affidavit or the extent to which the affidavit identifies the decedent as the source of funds or beneficial owner of the account.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-207.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.8.0">1973 c 619 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.16.0">1985 c 292 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.2.35.0">1995 c 207 art 2 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/217/#laws.2.19.0">1997 c 217 art 2 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-208">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-208 FINANCIAL INSTITUTION PROTECTION; PAYMENT ON SIGNATURE OF ONE PARTY.</h1>
        <p>Financial institutions may enter into multiple-party accounts to the same extent that they may enter into single-party accounts. Any multiple-party account may be paid, on request, to any one or more of the parties. A financial institution shall not be required to inquire as to the source of funds received for deposit to a multiple-party account, or to inquire as to the proposed application of any sum withdrawn from an account.</p>
        <p>A minor may be a party to a joint account.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-208.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.9.0">1973 c 619 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.17.0">1985 c 292 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-209">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-209 FINANCIAL INSTITUTION PROTECTION; PAYMENT AFTER DEATH OR DISABILITY; JOINT ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <p>Any sums in a joint account may be paid, on request, to any party without regard to whether any other party is incapacitated or deceased at the time the payment is demanded; but payment may not be made to the personal representative or heirs of a deceased party unless proofs of death are presented to the financial institution showing that the decedent was the last surviving party or unless there is no right of survivorship under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-204">524.6-204</a>, or unless a will provides other distribution; in which case the procedure set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-204">524.6-204</a>, clause (d), shall be followed. A minor may be a party to a joint account.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-209.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.10.0">1973 c 619 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.18.0">1985 c 292 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-210">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-210 FINANCIAL INSTITUTION PROTECTION; PAYMENT OF P.O.D. ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <p>Any P.O.D. account may be paid, on request, to any original party to the account. Payment of the interest of a P.O.D. payee may be made, on request, to the P.O.D. payee or to the personal representative or heirs of a deceased P.O.D. payee upon presentation to the financial institution of proof of death showing that the P.O.D. payee survived all persons named as original parties. Payment may be made to the personal representative or heirs of a deceased original party if proof of death is presented to the financial institution showing that the original party was the survivor of all other persons named on the account either as an original party or as P.O.D. payee.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-210.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.11.0">1973 c 619 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.19.0">1985 c 292 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-211">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-211 FINANCIAL INSTITUTION PROTECTION; DISCHARGE.</h1>
        <p>Payment made pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-208">524.6-208</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-210">524.6-210</a> discharges the financial institution from all claims for amounts so paid whether or not the payment is consistent with the beneficial ownership of the account as between parties, P.O.D. payees, or beneficiaries by will or otherwise, or their successors. The protection here given does not extend to payments made after a financial institution has received written notice from any person entitled to request payment to the effect that withdrawals in accordance with the terms of the account, including one having an agency designation, should not be permitted, and the financial institution has had a reasonable opportunity to act on it when the payment is made. Unless the notice is withdrawn by the person giving it, the successor of any deceased party and all other parties entitled to payment must concur in any demand for withdrawal if the financial institution is to be protected under this section. No other notice or any other information shown to have been available to a financial institution shall affect its right to the protection provided here. A financial institution that receives written notice pursuant to this section or that otherwise has reason to believe that a dispute exists as to the rights of the parties may refuse, without liability, to make payments in accordance with the terms of the account. The protection here provided shall not affect the rights of parties in disputes between themselves or their successors concerning the beneficial ownership of funds in, or withdrawn from, multiple-party accounts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-211.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.13.0">1973 c 619 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/292/#laws.0.20.0">1985 c 292 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/36/#laws.0.6.0">2013 c 36 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-212">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-212 FINANCIAL INSTITUTION PROTECTION; SETOFF.</h1>
        <p>Without qualifying any other statutory right to setoff or lien and subject to any contractual provision, if a party to a multiple-party account is indebted to a financial institution, the financial institution has a right to setoff against the account in which the party has or had immediately before death a present right of withdrawal. The amount of the account subject to setoff is that proportion to which the debtor is, or was immediately before death, beneficially entitled, and in the absence of proof of net contributions, to an equal share with all parties having present rights of withdrawal.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.524.6-212.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/619/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 619 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/472/#laws.0.63.0">1994 c 472 s 63</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.524.6-213">
        <h1 class="shn">524.6-213 FORMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-213.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-213.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Survivorship account.</span></h2>
          <p>Deposits made using a form of account containing the following language signed by the depositor shall be conclusive evidence of the intent of the depositor, in the absence of fraud or misrepresentation, subject, nevertheless, to other disposition made by will as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.6-204">524.6-204</a>, clause (d), to establish a survivorship account:</p>
          <p>(a) "I (we) direct that the balance remaining in this account shall be PAYABLE ON DEATH (of the survivor of us) to:</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:11.71875%">
            <col style="width:37.5%">
            <col style="width:13.28125%">
            <col style="width:37.5%">
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td></td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td></td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td style="text-align: center;">Signed:</td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
            </tr>
          </table>
          <p class="bl40">
            <span>Dated: </span>
            <span class="leader">.</span>
            <span>"</span>
          </p>
          <p>(b) "I (we) intend and agree that the balance in this account, upon the death of any party to this account, shall belong to the surviving party, or if there are two or more surviving parties, they shall take as JOINT TENANTS.</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:11.71875%">
            <col style="width:37.5%">
            <col style="width:13.28125%">
            <col style="width:37.5%">
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td style="text-align: center;">Signed:</td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
            </tr>
          </table>
          <p class="bl40">
            <span>Dated: </span>
            <span class="leader">.</span>
            <span>"</span>
          </p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-213.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-213.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Account subject to power of attorney with no survivorship rights.</span></h2>
          <p>Where no rights of survivorship are intended and the account is one to be established for convenience only between a depositor and an agent, the following language is recommended for use, and when so used, the account shall be construed as a matter of law to be an account subject to a power of attorney with no survivorship rights, the form to read as follows:</p>
          <p>"I ......................... (grantor of power), hereby constitute and appoint ......................... (grantee of power), as my attorney-in-fact, to deposit or withdraw funds held in ............... .......... (name of bank), in account No. .................</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:36.9230769230769%">
            <col style="width:13.0769230769231%">
            <col style="width:50%">
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td style="text-align: center;">Signed:</td>
              <td>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
            </tr>
          </table>
          <p>Dated:</p>
          <p>Acknowledgment: In the presence of ........................... (an authorized person), ............................ (name of financial institution)."</p>
          <p>The power so granted is subject to the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/508.72">508.72</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/508A.72">508A.72</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/523.01">523.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/523.24">523.24</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.524.6-213.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/524/full#stat.524.6-213.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contract of deposit; sample form.</span></h2>
          <p>A contract of deposit that contains provisions in substantially the following form establishes the type of account provided, and the account is governed by the provisions of this part applicable to an account of that type:</p>
          <p class="ce">UNIFORM SINGLE- OR MULTIPLE-PARTY ACCOUNT FORM</p>
